## D. Deep mathematics & machine learning — the transfer library (22)

### D1. Can LLMs infer causation from correlation? (Corr2Cause benchmark) — Jin et al. (@HochreiterSepp) (1668166039283159041) — LEARN-ONLY

- **What it is:** The Corr2Cause benchmark paper: LLMs score near-random on pure causal-inference tasks and succeed mainly on causal problems they have effectively already seen. Posted by Sepp Hochreiter with the conclusion stated bluntly.
- **Citation/links:** arXiv:2306.05836. **PDF: `arxiv-2306.05836.pdf`, 17 pp; abstract read.**
- **Use / system mapping:** A standing warning for the fleet's own reasoning doctrine: correlation-to-causation leaps are where LLM reasoning is weakest, so GSE's causal claims (scheme caused the split, the injury caused the collapse) need design-based support, not narrative.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Total — this is about the reasoning substrate every agent in the fleet runs on.
- **Autonomy + structure:** Causal claims in the reasoning trail should carry a design tag (RCT-like, natural experiment, adjustment set, or speculation).
- **Second-order:** With D2/D3, the causal shelf is now: benchmark of failure + two frontier methods papers.
- **Evidence:** arXiv metadata verified; abstract read.

### D2. Causal inference in staggered adoption panels: the comparison set depends on the intervention — Lundberg & Xu (@StatisticsME) (2114339671308452013) — LEARN-ONLY ★

- **What it is:** A 36-page methods paper on staggered-adoption designs (diff-in-diff, fixed effects, matching, synthetic control) showing the correct comparison set is determined by the intervention you mean — a subtle, consequential correction to how these designs are routinely run.
- **Citation/links:** arXiv:2610.31537. **PDF: `arxiv-2610.31537.pdf`, 36 pp; abstract read.**
- **Use / system mapping:** Staggered adoption is the shape of sports questions GSE actually faces: teams adopting a scheme/technology/coach at different times. Any "did X work" study in the corpus should be re-read against this paper's comparison-set logic.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** This is the current econometrics frontier — policy evaluation, product rollouts, medical adoption. One of the highest-transfer papers in the pull.
- **Autonomy + structure:** Study-design registry: estimand first, comparison set second, estimator third — in that order, as required fields.
- **Second-order:** Pairs with D1 as method vs warning; with D4 as the course-length foundation beneath both.
- **Evidence:** arXiv metadata verified; abstract read.

### D3. Causal inference in metric spaces via distance profiles — Song & Zhang (@StatisticsME) (2115146463768363202) — LEARN-ONLY

- **What it is:** A 51-page paper extending causal inference to outcomes that live in metric spaces (distributions, networks, trajectories) via distance profiles — treatment effects where the outcome isn't a number.
- **Citation/links:** arXiv:2610.33491 (stat.ME/stat.ML). **PDF: `arxiv-2610.33491.pdf`, 51 pp.**
- **Use / system mapping:** Sports outcomes are often trajectories and surfaces, not scalars (a drive, a route tree, a pass surface — cf. C2). This is the causal machinery for "did the intervention change the *shape* of play."
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Any field with functional/distributional outcomes — neuroimaging, climate fields, growth curves.
- **Autonomy + structure:** Names a gap in GSE's schema: effects are currently scalar-only; trajectory-valued effects need a home.
- **Second-order:** C2 (G2G surfaces) + D3 (causal effects on surfaces) = a complete research program in two dossiers.
- **Evidence:** arXiv resolved via post link; PDF downloaded.

### D4. A First Course in Causal Inference (490-page text, Ding) — @KirkDBorne (2114207572525801490) — ADOPT-CANDIDATE

- **What it is:** The full text of Peng Ding's *A First Course in Causal Inference* as a free ~490-page PDF on arXiv (post-stated page count) — potential outcomes, DAGs, and modern causal methods at course length, with Pearl's primer recommended alongside.
- **Citation/links:** arXiv:2305.18793. **PDF: `arxiv-2305.18793.pdf`** (downloaded; page count per pdfinfo in the manifest).
- **Use / system mapping:** The fleet's causal-inference reference text — the thing D1 says the models can't do unaided, written down at full length for the humans and agents checking them. ADOPT-CANDIDATE as the standing citation for causal questions in dossiers.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** The entire book is domain-free methodology.
- **Autonomy + structure:** Reference texts at full length, locally held — the corpus pattern this pull repeats (E-tier is the same bet at scale).
- **Second-order:** With D2, gives both the foundation and the frontier correction — read the frontier paper *against* the textbook chapter it revises.
- **Evidence:** arXiv resolved; PDF downloaded.

### D5. Bayesian neural networks via MCMC: a Python-based tutorial — @StatMLPapers (1828282213944762417) — LEARN-ONLY

- **What it is:** A 33-page tutorial paper teaching BNNs with full MCMC (not variational shortcuts) in Python — code-first uncertainty quantification for neural networks.
- **Citation/links:** arXiv:2304.02595. **PDF: `arxiv-2304.02595.pdf`, 33 pp.**
- **Use / system mapping:** The uncertainty discipline GSE's neural features lack: posterior predictive intervals from an actual sampler, with the tutorial's code as the on-ramp for a fleet agent to implement it.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** BNNs are the default answer to "neural net, but we need to know when it doesn't know" — medicine, engineering, finance.
- **Autonomy + structure:** Tutorial papers are agent-executable specs: an agent can run the code while reading — the ideal corpus format for skills transfer.
- **Second-order:** Contrasts with D11/D12 (variational methods): the corpus now holds both sides of the MCMC-vs-VI argument with working examples.
- **Evidence:** arXiv resolved; PDF downloaded.

### D6. Quasi-Bayesian Hierarchical Models — Fairall & Glinnan (@eBlogs) (2072218391054516528) — LEARN-ONLY

- **What it is:** A 100-page paper developing QBHM for grouped GMM settings: Bayesian hierarchical pooling where each group keeps its own objective function, with quasi-posterior asymptotics matching GMM under strong identification.
- **Citation/links:** arXiv:2606.31930 (econ.EM). **PDF: `arxiv-2606.31930.pdf`, 100 pp; abstract read.**
- **Use / system mapping:** Hierarchical pooling is GSE's house method (B4's kickers, C1's athletes); this is the theory of when pooling helps and when it contaminates — the fine print for the engine's favorite trick.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Grouped estimation is meta-analysis, multi-site trials, and multi-market forecasting — the paper is written for econometrics and reads across all of them.
- **Autonomy + structure:** Pooling decisions should cite an identification-strength check; this paper defines one.
- **Second-order:** The 100-page length matters: full proofs and conditions, the class of document the corpus is short on.
- **Evidence:** arXiv metadata verified; abstract read.

### D7. Behavioral mapping of hierarchical dynamical models accelerates Bayesian inference — Bai & Pandey (@tom_ohigashi) (2114251327383736792) — LEARN-ONLY

- **What it is:** A 17-page paper using behavioral mapping (matching a complex dynamical model's input-output behavior with a simpler surrogate behavior) to accelerate Bayesian parameter inference for hierarchical dynamical models.
- **Citation/links:** arXiv:2610.30683. **PDF: `arxiv-2610.30683.pdf`, 17 pp.**
- **Use / system mapping:** Inference speed is the practical bottleneck on every hierarchical model in this report (B4, C1, D6); surrogate-accelerated inference is how those models get re-fit weekly instead of once.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Dynamical-systems calibration is climate, epidemiology, and control engineering — the method is domain-free.
- **Autonomy + structure:** A pattern for the fleet: expensive true model + cheap behavioral surrogate + inference on the surrogate with error accounted.
- **Second-order:** D20 (deep GP surrogates) is the same bet in a different model family.
- **Evidence:** arXiv resolved; PDF downloaded.

### D8. Efficient deep Gaussian process surrogates via mini-batch latent MAP — Chang & Sung (@StatsPapers) (2116145914762948886) — LEARN-ONLY

- **What it is:** A 46-page paper (LaMP-DGP) replacing variational/MCMC inference in deep GPs with gradient-based latent MAP estimation on mini-batches — deep GP surrogates that scale to real computer-experiment workloads.
- **Citation/links:** arXiv:2610.38038 (stat.ML). **PDF: `arxiv-2610.38038.pdf`, 46 pp; abstract read.**
- **Use / system mapping:** Surrogate modeling is how expensive simulators become usable inside a reasoning loop — GSE's game simulator and any future CFD work (A-tier) both need surrogates; DGPs handle the nonstationary surfaces that plain GPs can't.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Computer-experiment emulation is engineering design, climate modeling, and materials science's core workflow.
- **Autonomy + structure:** Surrogate entries in the model registry should state the inference approximation used — this paper exists because that choice is consequential.
- **Second-order:** With D9/D10, the GP shelf now spans compartment models, space-time inference, and emulation — a full GP curriculum from one day's posts.
- **Evidence:** arXiv metadata verified; abstract read.

### D9. Compartment-driven Gaussian processes for pharmacokinetic UQ — Palta-Hill, Engelhardt, Tam (@StatAP_papers) (2116113088617291926) — LEARN-ONLY

- **What it is:** A 57-page paper (stat.AP) building GPs whose structure comes from pharmacokinetic compartment models, to quantify uncertainty in drug exposure (AUC, Cmax) — mechanistic structure + GP flexibility, aimed at efficacy/toxicity balance.
- **Citation/links:** arXiv:2610.38325. **PDF: `arxiv-2610.38325.pdf`, 57 pp; abstract read.**
- **Use / system mapping:** Filed for the *pattern*: domain-mechanistic kernels inside GP regression. The sports analog is immediate — mechanistic play models (drive physics, fatigue compartments) as GP structure rather than generic kernels.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** It IS the transfer example: a sports-pull paper whose home is drug development, included because the construction travels.
- **Autonomy + structure:** Kernel design as domain knowledge encoding — a feature-engineering doctrine entry.
- **Second-order:** Engelhardt's presence signals the genomics/ML crossover community; their UQ standards are worth importing wholesale.
- **Evidence:** arXiv metadata verified; abstract read.

### D10. Neural simulation-based inference for space-time Gaussian processes — Loret et al. (@StatisticsME) (2115567298844950712) — LEARN-ONLY

- **What it is:** A 52-page paper replacing prohibitive MLE for space-time GP covariance parameters with neural Bayes/ratio estimation (NBE/NRE) trained on simulations — Gneiting–Matérn fields as the testbed.
- **Citation/links:** arXiv:2610.36855 (stat.ME). **PDF: `arxiv-2610.36855.pdf`, 52 pp; abstract read.**
- **Use / system mapping:** Tracking data is space-time data; covariance structure (how separation decays with distance/time) is a real feature family for coverage and pressure surfaces. SBI is how you fit those models at NFL data scale.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** SBI is the rising default for simulator-based science (cosmology, climate, epidemiology) — a fleet-level method to know.
- **Autonomy + structure:** The pattern — simulator generates training pairs, network amortizes inference — is the same shape as A10's PINN detection and belongs in the methods registry as a named strategy.
- **Second-order:** With D8, covers both directions of the surrogate economy: emulate the model (D8), or emulate the inference (D10).
- **Evidence:** arXiv metadata verified; abstract read.

### D11. Rényi-regularized flow-based variational inference for Bayesian calibration — Ngarti et al. (@arnaudmercier) (2115725140201779210) — LEARN-ONLY ★

- **What it is:** A paper (Grenoble/Inria line) fixing a named failure of flow-based VI for Bayesian calibration: posteriors with modes separated by low-probability regions break the flow; a Rényi-regularized objective repairs it, demonstrated on a twin experiment.
- **Citation/links:** arXiv:2610.37169. **PDF: `arxiv-2610.37169.pdf`** (downloaded).
- **Use / system mapping:** The word "calibration" is literal: Bayesian calibration of simulators against observed data is what GSE does when it tunes the game simulator to history. Multimodal posteriors are the norm there (multiple scheme regimes), so this failure mode is ours.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Simulator calibration is engineering's digital-twin problem; the paper's home institutions are HPC/calibration groups, not sports.
- **Autonomy + structure:** Calibration pipelines should test for multimodality before trusting a variational posterior — a checklist item this paper earns.
- **Second-order:** A10 (PINN detection) + D11 (calibration) + D12 (VI limits) triangulate the same question from three sides: how do we know the approximation is honest?
- **Evidence:** arXiv resolved; abstract read (in-post full text).

### D12. Acceleration and its limits in Gaussian variational inference — Park, Jang, Chen (@StatsPapers) (2111136015851155715) — LEARN-ONLY

- **What it is:** A 52-page theory paper: momentum acceleration's √κ improvement extends to Gaussian VI with exact expectations — and a construction showing it *fails* when only pointwise derivatives are available. A theorem and its boundary in one paper.
- **Citation/links:** arXiv:2610.21020 (stat.ML/math.OC). **PDF: `arxiv-2610.21020.pdf`, 52 pp; abstract read.**
- **Use / system mapping:** VI is the fleet's likely inference engine at scale (D5's MCMC won't always be affordable); knowing exactly where acceleration breaks is operational knowledge, not trivia.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Pure optimization/inference theory — transfers everywhere VI is used.
- **Autonomy + structure:** "Method + its proven limit" pairs are the corpus's most valuable paper shape; dossiers should always extract the limit, not just the claim.
- **Second-order:** With D11, the VI shelf has both a fix and a fence.
- **Evidence:** arXiv metadata verified; abstract read.

### D13. Variational Particle Approximations (JMLR) — @tejasdkulkarni (914504516892004352) — LEARN-ONLY

- **What it is:** A JMLR paper announcement for a simple inference algorithm — variational particle approximations — from the probabilistic-programming research line (Kulkarni et al.), an older but foundational alternative in the VI/particle family.
- **Citation/links:** JMLR paper (post is the pointer; journal version is the source).
- **Use / system mapping:** Historical depth for the inference shelf: particle variational methods are ancestors of several modern samplers; having the lineage on file keeps the corpus from treating 2026 methods as unmotivated inventions.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Inference algorithms are domain-free by definition.
- **Autonomy + structure:** None direct; methods-lineage entry.
- **Second-order:** Connects to E12 (Rainforth's probprog thesis) — same research neighborhood, tutorial form.
- **Evidence:** as-posted; journal paper not downloaded (not an arXiv-pointer post).

### D14. Belief-Conditioned Multi-Agent Actor Critic for decentralized LLM collaboration — Chen, Liu, Amato (@SciFi) (2116052318127071285) — LEARN-ONLY ★

- **What it is:** A NeurIPS 2026 workshop paper applying multi-agent RL (belief-conditioned actor-critic) to decentralized LLM collaboration — agents that must infer what teammates know because communication is limited and workflows aren't predefined.
- **Citation/links:** arXiv:2610.35398 (cs.AI/cs.MA). **PDF: `arxiv-2610.35398.pdf`, 18 pp; abstract read.**
- **Use / system mapping:** This is the fleet's own coordination problem stated as research: Motif/Jules/AGY/Hermes are decentralized agents with partial views. Belief-conditioning — modeling what other agents know — is a concrete upgrade idea for fleet task routing.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** The paper's subject *is* agent systems; sports relevance is nil and fleet relevance is direct — the mission lens in reverse.
- **Autonomy + structure:** Fleet protocols could carry explicit "belief" fields (what this agent believes others have verified) — a schema this paper motivates.
- **Second-order:** Read beside the bus-heartbeat finding (one heartbeat ever): our coordination substrate is primitive next to the research frontier, and this paper maps the direction.
- **Evidence:** arXiv metadata verified; abstract read.

### D15. Best Policy Tracking in reinforcement learning — Echevarrieta et al. (@Memoirs) (2115637198141735305) — LEARN-ONLY

- **What it is:** A TMLR-submitted paper (40 pp) on best-policy tracking — following the best policy as the environment drifts, rather than converging once to a fixed optimum.
- **Citation/links:** arXiv:2610.34501; OpenReview forum linked. **PDF: `arxiv-2610.34501.pdf`, 40 pp.**
- **Use / system mapping:** Non-stationarity is the NFL's native condition (rosters, rules, schemes drift weekly); a tracking formulation fits in-season model updating better than the train-once frame most public models use.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Any deployed policy under drift (pricing, recommendations, control) has this problem.
- **Autonomy + structure:** Model-update cadence should be a designed parameter (tracking) not an accident (whenever someone re-runs the notebook).
- **Second-order:** With C4 (RL discovering tactics), covers RL for sports from both ends: design new strategies, and keep them current.
- **Evidence:** arXiv resolved; PDF downloaded.

### D16. RobustRLlib: a unified library and benchmark for robust RL — Chen et al. (@Memoirs) (2115601320010752231) — TEST

- **What it is:** A 48-page paper + open library benchmarking robust reinforcement learning algorithms under a single harness — robustness (performance under perturbation) as the measured property, with code public.
- **Citation/links:** arXiv:2610.32971; robustrl-lib.github.io. **PDF: `arxiv-2610.32971.pdf`, 48 pp.**
- **Use / system mapping:** TEST because it's runnable: benchmark harnesses are how the fleet should evaluate any RL component before trusting it (the Laya lesson: candidates audition on a frozen harness or they don't audition). RobustRLlib is a template for that harness shape.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Robust control is robotics' and process control's core need.
- **Autonomy + structure:** The library+benchmark pairing is the adoption unit: code without a benchmark is a claim.
- **Second-order:** Its robustness framing disciplines C4's enthusiasm: discovered tactics must survive perturbation to count.
- **Evidence:** arXiv resolved; project page linked.

### D17. CDE: Curiosity-Driven Exploration for RL in LLMs — Dai et al. (@1MoNo2Prod) (2115716964769534161) — LEARN-ONLY

- **What it is:** A 21-page paper formalizing curiosity (sign-based intrinsic reward) to fix RLVR's poor exploration and entropy collapse when training LLM reasoning.
- **Citation/links:** arXiv:2509.09675. **PDF: `arxiv-2509.09675.pdf`, 21 pp; abstract read.**
- **Use / system mapping:** Exploration failure is also a *research-process* failure: sweeps and model searches collapse onto the familiar. CDE's formalism (curiosity as a measurable, rewardable quantity) is a lens for designing the fleet's own search behavior — including this sweep's keyword program.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** RLVR training is the AI industry's central workload right now; the method is native to it.
- **Autonomy + structure:** Search processes should log exploration breadth as a metric (queries tried vs queries that returned), which this pull's gap audit now does informally.
- **Second-order:** Meta-level pairing with D1: one paper on what LLMs can't infer, one on how to make them explore better anyway.
- **Evidence:** arXiv metadata verified; abstract read.

### D18. Test-time scaling of diffusions with flow maps — Albergo et al. (@msalbergo) (1995583072482537799) — LEARN-ONLY

- **What it is:** A NeurIPS paper (35 pp) on adapting diffusion models at inference time with rewards, done correctly at the level of the flow map (trajectory tilting), with a public project page.
- **Citation/links:** arXiv:2511.22688; flow-map-trajectory-tilting.github.io. **PDF: `arxiv-2511.22688.pdf`, 35 pp.**
- **Use / system mapping:** Test-time compute allocation is the live question in every agent workflow (how much thinking per decision); this is the diffusion-side theory of spending inference compute against a reward — the same trade the fleet makes with model tiers.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Generative modeling infrastructure — domain-free.
- **Autonomy + structure:** "Adapt at the level of the whole trajectory, not the step" is a design principle that maps onto agent planning, not just sampling.
- **Second-order:** With A10 (PINNs for stopping boundaries), the pull's generative/decision theory shelf is coherent: sample, tilt, stop.
- **Evidence:** arXiv resolved; PDF downloaded.

### D19. Scaling Laws for Neural Language Models — Kaplan et al. (@cloneofsimo) (1850718242140221947) — LEARN-ONLY

- **What it is:** The scaling-laws paper (arXiv:2001.08361, 30 pp), posted as a corrective: loss scales as a power law in model/data/compute, which is why architecture tweaks rarely produce dramatic gains — the post's point is that most "fundamental" transformer questions were settled by this empirical law.
- **Citation/links:** arXiv:2001.08361. **PDF: `arxiv-2001.08361.pdf`, 30 pp; abstract read.**
- **Use / system mapping:** Calibration for fleet expectations: capability planning should run on scaling-style empirical laws (measure the curve, extrapolate honestly) rather than architecture hope — the same epistemics as GSE's measurement bars.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Power-law resource planning appears in any maturing empirical field (including sports model accuracy vs data volume — a genuinely open question for tracking data).
- **Autonomy + structure:** Filed as a doctrine reference: when a claim promises a dramatic jump, demand the scaling curve.
- **Second-order:** The post is also a corpus-culture artifact: a researcher telling newcomers to read the paper before opining — the behavior this sweep exists to import.
- **Evidence:** arXiv metadata verified; abstract read.

### D20. Transcripts to transcendence: LLMs learning chess beyond their data — @AlexGDimakis (1803293833889042637) — LEARN-ONLY ★

- **What it is:** A paper summary with the mechanism explained: an LLM trained only on game transcripts of ≤1000-Elo players plays at ~1500 Elo — performance beyond every demonstrator — explained as ensembling weak learners (averaging independent mistakes), contingent on data diversity and temperature sampling.
- **Citation/links:** post text (paper discussed; Dimakis's summary is the content).
- **How to use it / system mapping:** The theoretically-cleanest statement in the pull of why ensembles beat their members — directly relevant to GSE's multi-model/simulator ensembles and to the fleet's multi-agent review design (independent errors are the fuel; correlated reviewers add nothing).
- **Transfer beyond sports:** This is a general theorem-shaped insight about learning from weak supervision — annotation, crowdsourcing, and distillation all live here.
- **Autonomy + structure:** Reviewer/ensemble design rule, now with a citation: diversity of the members is a measurable requirement, not a vibe.
- **Second-order:** Explains B13's "Zoo" from first principles, five years early and in another field.
- **Evidence:** as-posted expert summary; underlying paper not separately resolved (gap).

### D21. DPO's foundations revisited: the Bradley–Terry coupling isn't fundamental — ICML 2026 spotlight (@Pyuyi2333) (2090264770146443586) — LEARN-ONLY

- **What it is:** A Google DeepMind/Research ICML26 spotlight paper showing DPO's usual coupling to the Bradley–Terry–Luce choice model and convex logistic losses is far less fundamental than assumed — preference learning works under weaker assumptions than the standard derivation suggests.
- **Citation/links:** arXiv:2507.07855. **PDF: `arxiv-2507.07855.pdf`, 24 pp.**
- **Use / system mapping:** Bradley–Terry is a GSE rating engine component (local-gse-rating-engines covers BT) *and* the load-bearing model of LLM preference training; a foundations revision touches both shelves at once — exactly the kind of paper the mission lens exists to catch.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** The paper is about AI alignment machinery; the transfer runs AI → sports ratings here, the reverse of the usual direction.
- **Autonomy + structure:** When a foundational assumption is revised, dependent systems get a re-check flag — BT usage in the rating engines should cite which assumptions it actually needs.
- **Second-order:** C13 (BT rankings can flip) + D21 (BT assumptions are looser than believed) = the corpus's BT position is now genuinely two-sided and current.
- **Evidence:** arXiv resolved; PDF downloaded.

### D22. Bayesian Treed Vecchia Gaussian Process emulation via SMC — Ye & Ma (@StatisticsCO) (2115328378135855508) — LEARN-ONLY

- **What it is:** A 50-page paper (stat.CO) combining treed GP partitioning, Vecchia approximations, and sequential Monte Carlo for GP emulation of expensive simulators — three scalability tricks composed.
- **Citation/links:** arXiv:2610.36770. **PDF: `arxiv-2610.36770.pdf`, 50 pp.**
- **Use / system mapping:** Emulation is the bridge between the fleet's expensive simulators (game sim, flight physics) and fast reasoning loops; Vecchia-style sparsity is the standard route to GP inference at tracking-data scale.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Simulator emulation is the digital-twin industry core method.
- **Autonomy + structure:** Composition note: scalable methods are usually *stacks* of approximations — dossiers should name each layer so error can be budgeted layer by layer.
- **Second-order:** Completes the GP arc with D8/D9/D10: four different answers to "GPs don't scale," each attacking a different term.
- **Evidence:** arXiv resolved; PDF downloaded.

- **Transfer beyond sports:** High-dimensional statistics is the mathematics of genomics, finance, and all sensor-rich science.

## Synthesis — what this pull changes

**The physics tier is real and underpriced.** Fourteen of the fifteen A-tier dossiers concern one phenomenon family — surface features (seams, dimples, panels) breaking the clean drag/Magnus model — appearing independently in baseball, soccer, golf, and fluid-mechanics venues, plus a Navier-Stokes-benchmark PINN paper whose actual lesson is evaluation design (A11: global metrics certify the easy region). The fleet's physics lane (EQ-PHYSICS-001, MOD-PHYSICS-001) has public literature to stand on now, most of it downloaded.

**The transfer shelf is the deepest it has ever been.** D2 (staggered-adoption causal inference), D6 (quasi-Bayesian hierarchy, 100 pp), D8–D10/D22 (four different answers to "GPs don't scale"), and D11/D12 (VI calibration and its proven limits) are not sports content at all — they are the methods a research-and-data company runs on, found through the sports door exactly as the mission lens predicts. D14 turns the lens around: multi-agent RL for decentralized LLM collaboration is research about *the fleet itself*.

**The reference library grew by ~4,600 pages in one pull.** E-tier books and notes (NumPy 371, deep-learning theory 737 + 333, BDA, MML 417, high-dimensional statistics 168, probability 386 + 117, optimization 129) are now local files an agent can cite by page — the difference between a corpus that *mentions* theory and one that *holds* it.

**Provenance got external validation.** F7 (a football-docs platform whose agent verifies quotes against sources before citing) is Garrett's citation doctrine implemented independently — evidence the standard is both right and buildable.

## Top 10 finds

1. **A3 — Spinning baseball physics notes (Pelletier, 9 pp, read)** — rigid-body precession as the seam-reorientation mechanism; the NumPy-PDF class, in the priority physics tier.
2. **A11 — PINN regional accuracy on the lid-driven cavity (arXiv:2610.38814, 39 pp)** — Navier-Stokes benchmark + the worst-region evaluation doctrine.
3. **C5 — Expected Possession Value, full technical version (arXiv:2012.05757, 39 pp)** — the EP lineage at full length, local.
4. **D2 — Staggered-adoption causal inference (arXiv:2610.31537, 36 pp)** — the comparison-set correction; maximum transfer.
5. **E6 — MIT High-Dimensional Statistics notes (arXiv:2310.19244, 168 pp)** — the theory of the tracking-data regime.
6. **E2 — Mathematical Introduction to Deep Learning (arXiv:2310.20360, 737 pp)** — the heaviest document in the pull.
7. **F7 — football docs v0.16 quote-verifying research agent** — provenance doctrine, implemented by others.
8. **B4 — Hierarchical field-goal model spec (23,247 kicks)** — the missing DTW-v2 component, publicly blueprinted.
9. **C1 — Athlete trajectories + latent peak ages (arXiv:2610.35473, 30 pp)** — projection-layer mathematics.
10. **D14 — Belief-Conditioned Multi-Agent Actor Critic (arXiv:2610.35398)** — the fleet's coordination problem as published research.

## Gap audit (completion standard — what was NOT done, with counts)

**Not read at content level**
- The **249 overflow posts** above: inventoried from the search render only, not dossiered. They were deliberately left OUT of the seen index so future runs can still take them.
- Downloaded PDFs: **48 files, 4,567 pages**. Read in full: none at book length. Read at abstract + structure level: all 28 arXiv-API-resolved papers. Read at content level (multiple pages): A3 spinning-ball notes. The E-tier books (737 + 677 + 490 + 417 + 386 pp…) are held, page-verified, and skimmed at front matter — they are reference holdings, not read books, and this report does not pretend otherwise.
- Pointer-only counted finds whose underlying document was not read: **9** (A2 blog via unexpanded t.co; A7 paper not named in the render; C8 full text; C12 book; D13 JMLR paper; E4 PDF sits behind the book page; E11 notes link lost in the render; E12 thesis via t.co; F17 thesis via t.co).

**Not returned**
- **2 of 52 searches failed on first attempt** (p07 golf aero, p08 track physics — helper HTTP error) and were re-run in full; net unreturned searches: **0**.
- The pool's **58 unexpanded t.co links** belong overwhelmingly to overflow posts; every link a counted dossier depends on was expanded or replaced by its resolved primary source, except the 9 pointer-gaps named above.
- @FTNData remains protected (0 posts) — not part of this pull's program, noted for continuity with prior runs.

**Not verified**
- Every as-posted number in every dossier (make rates, pressure splits, Elo deltas, fixture counts) is UNVERIFIED unless the dossier says otherwise. What WAS verified: post IDs and texts as rendered; arXiv metadata (titles/authors/dates) for 28 papers via the arXiv API; PDF existence and page counts for 48 files via pdfinfo; publisher/university/Kaggle/GitHub landing pages for the F-tier and A6/A14.
- No model, repo, or dataset in this pull was executed or reproduced. TEST verdicts (A4, A12, C2, C8, C13, D16, F1, F11, F15) name the first test for each; none has been run yet.

**Chart-locked**
- **67 of the 100 counted posts carry images.** In **3** counted dossiers the decision-relevant values live in the image and are flagged as gaps in-line (A4 model chart, A7 paper summary without the paper, B6 pressure chart). In the other 64, the substance was carried by text or by the linked primary source.
- In the wider pool, chart-first posts from watchlist accounts (weekly EPA/pressure/separation charts) were the largest single class rejected at triage — their values are not readable on this route, and at this pull's bar they did not displace document-carrying posts.

**Exclusions (counted, never opened or never counted)**
- Promo/pick-seller posts in the returned pool: **4** (3 SumerPass subscription promos from the SumerSports methods-pull, 1 pick-seller CLV explainer). None opened beyond the render line needed to classify them.
- Same-artifact duplicates within the pull: **4** (the NumPy guide posted 3× by @KirkDBorne — counted once at E1; the BNN-via-MCMC tutorial posted 2× — counted once at D5; a near-duplicate CFDSUPPORT dimple post — counted once at A8).
- Health/injury posts: **0 counted** (none in the pool tied availability to a model or feature; the class barely surfaced on a math-first program).

**Route honesty**
- This is a search reconstruction of X via grok-4.3/x_search, not a scroll of the signed-in For You timeline, and not a browser session. Where academic channels (arXiv API, publisher pages, university sites, Kaggle, GitHub) beat the post for the primary source, the dossier says so. A separate lane owns the 50-PDF hunt; the 48 PDFs here are only the ones counted posts pointed at.

# PART 3 — RESEARCH PAPERS (50 PDFs)

# Research PDF Hunt — 50 Doctoral-Grade Papers (2026-10-30)

**Order:** Garrett, Oct 30 17:25 CT — 50 research PDFs, doctoral-grade provenance only, with the Oct 30 17:29 CT mission lens: GSE is a research and data company that proves its mathematics on sports; sports is the dataset and the proving ground; the prize is math that transfers beyond sports.

## Headline counts
- **PDFs downloaded and verified: 50** (each file opened, page count verified with pdfinfo, full text extracted)
- **Total pages: 1549**
- **Verdicts:** ADOPT 22, ADAPT 15, LEARN-ONLY 13
- **STRUCTURE-flagged (datasets/corpora/harnesses): 11**
- **Topic spread:** Calibration / conformal: 4; Probabilistic programming: 3; Rating systems: 3; Deep learning theory: 3; Model tuning / Bayesian optimization: 3; Physics-informed ML / operators: 2; Sports statistics: 2; Deep learning efficiency: 2; Deep learning architectures: 2; Scaling / training dynamics: 2; Alignment / preference learning: 2; Datasets / formal mathematics: 2; Agents: 2; Physics-informed ML: 1; Physics-informed ML / simulation: 1; Equation discovery / dynamics: 1; Dynamics / deep learning: 1; Bayesian inference: 1; Bayesian inference / MCMC: 1; Deep learning training: 1; Reasoning / evaluation: 1; Evaluation / judging: 1; Evaluation: 1; Model tuning / bandits: 1; Model tuning / systems: 1; Datasets / verifiers: 1; Datasets / benchmarks: 1; Datasets / code evaluation: 1; Datasets / agent benchmarks: 1; Datasets / frontier evaluation: 1; Agents / software engineering: 1

## Method and provenance bar
Every counted item clears at least one of: named authors with a verifiable university/lab/institution affiliation (checked against the affiliation lines printed on the PDF's first pages), an institutional technical-report series (e.g., INRIA RR-7445), or a peer-reviewed venue with a program/journal record (NeurIPS, ICML, ICLR, JASA, JMLR, PNAS, Nature Machine Intelligence, JCP, TMLR, CVPR, MLSys). Creator-economy PDFs, lead magnets, and anonymous material were excluded by class. Dedupe was by arXiv ID against a union set of **4,057 IDs** built from `~/workspace/arxiv-sweep/existing-research-map.md`, all arXiv-sweep tracker/selection JSONL files, and the full-text corpus filenames — IDs, not headline counts (the record's 579-read vs 750-tracker figures conflict, so neither was trusted). Each paper was read at full-text level: abstract, methods, and key results sections reviewed from the extracted text.

## Reference find — Garrett's OpenAI repository
**Identified: `openai/math` — https://github.com/openai/math** (owner: OpenAI; public; Apache-2.0; created 2026-10-06). Per the repository's own README (read 2026-10-30): it contains mathematical manuscripts and supporting proof artifacts produced by an unnamed internal OpenAI model; the current catalogue is **721 manuscripts organized into 373 result families**; over the evaluation the model was **posed approximately 4,000 problems**; each result used on average ~3 hours of ChatGPT Pro-level thinking compute; **~42% of top-line results are formalized in Lean**; the repo root ships an `overview.pdf`, a `preprints/` directory of per-manuscript PDFs with BibTeX blocks, a Lean library, and abridged reasoning summaries for 10 families. **Precision note:** Garrett's recollection of 'roughly 1,500 equations solved during testing' does not match the repo's own published counts (721 manuscripts / 373 families / ~4,000 posed) — no other OpenAI repository matching the description was found, and this is the only OpenAI repo of solved-problem manuscripts at corpus scale, so it is recorded as the reference find with the count discrepancy stated rather than smoothed over. It does not ship a single PDF of the whole corpus, so it is documented here rather than counted among the 50. (The separate September 2026 Navier-Stokes claim in Garrett's record — a different, single-problem effort — is not this repository.)

## ARGON material located in Garrett's corpus (flagged per brief)
- `memory/2026-10-15.md` (lines 5 and 181): **'Argon' is Garrett's name for Hermes** during the Oct 15, 2026 overnight QC/QA campaign ('Hermes ("Argon") is running HEAVY math and still running').
- `~/workspace/your_files/argon-rollup-audit-adjudication-2026-10-15.md`: Motif's claim-by-claim adjudication of the uploaded 'ARGON Walk-Forward Rollup' audit DOCX — the largest ARGON artifact in the record.
- `memory/2026-10-15.md` (line 288): Hermes residual artifacts referenced by name — `research/argon-rollup-2026-10-15.json`, `line_movement_pit_safe` files, `conformal.py` — **not found by name anywhere in the reachable workspace corpus** at that time.
- Grok thread 'Argon Deep Research Adjustment and CRPS Gap Analysis' (grok.com, per `memory/2026-10-15.md` lines 495–522) — 'Argon' is also that thread's name in the record.
- Navier-Stokes harvest: `memory/2026-10-17.md` (line 422): on 2026-10-17, 800 arXiv papers were cataloged at commit `8d88883a4` on branch `research/layer2-weeks1-5-reasoning-ingestion-2026-10-16` — 400 prediction-model sports papers and **400 Navier-Stokes / fluid-mechanics papers** — adding registry entries **EQ-PHYSICS-001** (NS drag with air density as a function of altitude and temperature) and **MOD-PHYSICS-001** (Runge-Kutta atmospheric drag / Magnus trajectory simulator — a specification in models.md, not running code). That harvest is catalog-class (cataloged, not deep-read). The physics papers in §1 of this report are canonical methods papers read here at full-text/methods level; they are not recounted from that catalog (see Gap Audit for the overlap caveat).
- No standalone 'ARGON engine' dataset file exists in the searched corpus; per Garrett's Oct 30 note, ARGON is his spoken name for the engine line whose dataset pairs with the sports dataset. Nothing about ARGON has been invented in this report.

## 1 — Physics of sport → open physics (the ARGON door)

### 1. Fourier Neural Operator for Parametric Partial Differential Equations
- **Citation:** Zongyi Li, Nikola Kovachki, Kamyar Azizzadenesheli, Burigede Liu, Kaushik Bhattacharya, Andrew Stuart, Anima Anandkumar — Caltech and Purdue University. ICLR (2021), 2020. arXiv:2010.08895 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.08895 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2010.08895v3
- **File:** [Li_2020_fourier_neural_operator_parametric_partial.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Li_2020_fourier_neural_operator_parametric_partial.pdf) — **16 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **STRUCTURE:** Operator-learning architecture — a reusable surrogate layer for any PDE-governed GSE feature.
- **What it proves/builds:** Fourier Neural Operator learns solution operators for whole families of PDEs, including Navier-Stokes, at a fraction of classical solver cost, with resolution invariance.
- **Why it matters:** This is the ARGON door: a learned operator can turn tracking/trajectory data into fast fluid/trajectory simulation — and the same operator idea prices how a ball, a puck, or a player moves through air and contact.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** ARGON/sports-physics lane; GSE trajectory and tracking features; signal pack (physics features)
- **Second-order uses:** FNO surrogate inside a Bayesian loop (NumPyro) yields uncertainty-aware physics features; with SINDy (1509.03580) you can audit what the operator learned against sparse governing terms.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Directly: weather, aerodynamics, reservoir and cardiovascular flow, materials. This is world-math, proven on sports-shaped data.
- **Verdict:** ADOPT — **First use:** Reproduce the paper's Navier-Stokes vorticity benchmark at small scale, then fine-tune the operator framing on baseball/football trajectory snippets.

### 2. Physics Informed Deep Learning (Part I): Data-driven Solutions of Nonlinear Partial Differential Equations
- **Citation:** Maziar Raissi, Paris Perdikaris, George Em Karniadakis — Brown University (Applied Mathematics) and University of Pennsylvania. Journal of Computational Physics (2019), 2017. arXiv:1711.10561 — https://arxiv.org/abs/1711.10561 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1711.10561v1
- **File:** [Raissi_2017_physics_informed_deep_learning_part.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Raissi_2017_physics_informed_deep_learning_part.pdf) — **22 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** Physics-Informed Neural Networks, Part I (Raissi, Perdikaris, Karniadakis — Brown/UPenn): embed PDE residuals into the loss so networks solve forward and inverse problems from sparse data, demonstrated on Burgers and Schrödinger equations.
- **Why it matters:** The original PINN paper and the cleanest statement of the ARGON thesis: physics you know becomes training signal for data you have. Ball flight (drag + Magnus) is a low-dimensional PDE/ODE problem begging for this treatment.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** ARGON/sports-physics lane (ball-flight and trajectory models); GSE weather/totals physics features
- **Second-order uses:** PINN ball-flight + MOD-PHYSICS-001's Runge-Kutta drag/Magnus simulator: the classical simulator generates labels, the PINN inverts them from observed tracking — each teaches the other, exactly Garrett's pairing.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Inverse problems across engineering: subsurface flow, biomedical, aerospace. A genuinely world-facing method.
- **Verdict:** ADOPT — **First use:** Build the smallest PINN: infer drag/Magnus coefficients from public pitch-trajectory data and compare to the RK simulator spec in MOD-PHYSICS-001.

### 3. Learning to Simulate Complex Physics with Graph Networks
- **Citation:** Alvaro Sanchez-Gonzalez, Jonathan Godwin, Tobias Pfaff, Rex Ying, Jure Leskovec, Peter W. Battaglia — DeepMind. ICML (2020), 2020. arXiv:2002.09405 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2002.09405 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2002.09405v2
- **File:** [Sanchez-Gonzalez_2020_learning_simulate_complex_physics_graph.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Sanchez-Gonzalez_2020_learning_simulate_complex_physics_graph.pdf) — **20 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **STRUCTURE:** Learned-simulator architecture: encoder-processor-decoder GNN as a reusable simulation layer.
- **What it proves/builds:** Learning to Simulate (DeepMind): graph-network simulators learn complex physics (fluids, rigid bodies, deformables) and roll out stable long trajectories, generalizing across materials (ICML 2020).
- **Why it matters:** This is the second ARGON door: learned simulators from trajectory data. Player/ball tracking is trajectory data; a GNN simulator is a candidate engine for play development and counterfactuals.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** ARGON/sports-physics lane; tracking-data modeling; signal pack (play-evolution features)
- **Second-order uses:** GNS rollouts + SINDy (1509.03580) audits: simulate with the graph net, interrogate with sparse equation discovery; FNO (2010.08895) covers the continuum/PDE side.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Robotics, materials, weather, games/VFX — learned simulation is a general technology.
- **Verdict:** ADOPT — **First use:** Reproduce the paper's smallest fluid benchmark, then scope a football-trajectory analogue (pass rush / route development) with available tracking samples.

### 4. DeepONet: Learning nonlinear operators for identifying differential equations based on the universal approximation theorem of operators
- **Citation:** Lu Lu, Pengzhan Jin, George Em Karniadakis — Brown University, Division of Applied Mathematics. Nature Machine Intelligence (2021), 2019. arXiv:1910.03193 — https://arxiv.org/abs/1910.03193 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1910.03193v3 · DOI: 10.1038/s42256-021-00302-5
- **File:** [Lu_2019_deeponet_learning_nonlinear_operators_identifying.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Lu_2019_deeponet_learning_nonlinear_operators_identifying.pdf) — **22 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** DeepONet (Lu, Karniadakis et al. — Brown): branch/trunk networks learn nonlinear operators between function spaces, grounded in a universal approximation theorem for operators (Nature Machine Intelligence).
- **Why it matters:** The sibling of FNO for the physics lane: where FNO is grid/Fourier-native, DeepONet handles scattered sensors — which is what sports tracking data actually is.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** ARGON/sports-physics lane; tracking-data modeling
- **Second-order uses:** DeepONet for operator regression from tracking points + FNO (2010.08895) for field-level simulation: the pair covers both data geometries the physics lane will meet.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Digital twins, materials design, geophysics — operator learning is a general scientific technology.
- **Verdict:** ADAPT — **First use:** Write the operator-learning comparison note (FNO vs DeepONet) keyed to GSE's two data shapes: gridded fields vs scattered tracking.

### 5. Discovering governing equations from data: Sparse identification of nonlinear dynamical systems
- **Citation:** Steven L. Brunton, Joshua L. Proctor, J. Nathan Kutz — University of Washington and Institute for Disease Modeling. PNAS (2016), 2015. arXiv:1509.03580 — https://arxiv.org/abs/1509.03580 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1509.03580v1 · DOI: 10.1073/pnas.1517384113
- **File:** [Brunton_2015_discovering_governing_equations_data_sparse.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Brunton_2015_discovering_governing_equations_data_sparse.pdf) — **26 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** SINDy (Brunton, Proctor, Kutz — U Washington): sparse regression over a library of candidate terms discovers governing ODEs/PDEs from data, demonstrated on fluid and chaotic systems (PNAS 2016).
- **Why it matters:** This is MOVE-37's flagship mathematics in published form: machines proposing the actual equations. Sports trajectories, possession dynamics, and fatigue curves are all candidate dynamical systems.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** MOVE-37 machine-discovery lane; ARGON physics lane; GSE dynamics features
- **Second-order uses:** SINDy + PINNs (1711.10561): SINDy proposes sparse equations, PINNs fit the ones with known structure — a discovery/confirmation pair for the physics lane. MOVE-37's own atlas already ranks SINDy-family methods; this is the primary source.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Equation discovery for any measured dynamical system — biology, climate, engineering. World-math by design.
- **Verdict:** ADOPT — **First use:** Run SINDy on a public ball-trajectory dataset and see which drag/Magnus terms it selects versus the MOD-PHYSICS-001 spec.

### 6. Neural Ordinary Differential Equations
- **Citation:** Ricky T. Q. Chen, Yulia Rubanova, Jesse Bettencourt, David Duvenaud — University of Toronto and Vector Institute. NeurIPS (2018), 2018. arXiv:1806.07366 — https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.07366 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1806.07366v6
- **File:** [Chen_2018_neural_ordinary_differential_equations.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Chen_2018_neural_ordinary_differential_equations.pdf) — **19 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** Neural ODEs (Chen, Duvenaud et al. — Toronto/Vector): parameterize the derivative of a continuous-depth network and solve with ODE solvers, enabling continuous-time models and irregular time series (NeurIPS 2018, best paper).
- **Why it matters:** Sports time is irregular (plays, possessions, injuries); continuous-time latent dynamics fit GSE better than fixed-step recurrence for several problems.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** GSE engine (continuous-time player/team dynamics); ARGON lane
- **Second-order uses:** Neural ODE + SINDy: learn the vector field neurally, then distill it sparsely — a two-stage discovery pipeline for MOVE-37.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Scientific ML, medical time series, finance — continuous-time modeling broadly.
- **Verdict:** ADAPT — **First use:**  prototype a latent-ODE on quarter-by-quarter team strength trajectories and compare interpolation error vs discrete baselines.

## 2 — Bayesian machinery: inference, probabilistic programming, MCMC

### 7. Variational Inference: A Review for Statisticians
- **Citation:** David M. Blei, Alp Kucukelbir, Jon D. McAuliffe — Columbia University (CS & Statistics) and UC Berkeley. Journal of the American Statistical Association (2017), 2016. arXiv:1601.00670 — https://arxiv.org/abs/1601.00670 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1601.00670v9 · DOI: 10.1080/01621459.2017.1285773
- **File:** [Blei_2016_variational_inference_review_statisticians.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Blei_2016_variational_inference_review_statisticians.pdf) — **41 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** A complete, statistician-grade map of variational inference: ELBO optimization, mean-field and structured approximations, stochastic VI, and diagnostics for when a fast approximation is trustworthy.
- **Why it matters:** GSE's hierarchical models need posterior speed without giving up auditability; VI is the scaling path when MCMC is too slow for nightly refits.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** GSE engine math (hierarchical team/player models); calibration lane refit pipeline
- **Second-order uses:** Pair with NUTS (1111.4246): VI for screening, MCMC for final numbers — a two-tier inference budget across the whole model zoo.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** General Bayesian workflow far beyond sports: finance, epidemiology, and any hierarchical forecasting shop.
- **Verdict:** ADOPT — **First use:** Benchmark stochastic VI vs NUTS on one existing hierarchical rating model; report posterior-mean drift and interval coverage.

### 8. Probabilistic Programming in Python using PyMC
- **Citation:** John Salvatier, Thomas Wiecki, Christopher Fonnesbeck — PeerJ Computer Science authors: Salvatier; Wiecki (Quantopian); Fonnesbeck (Vanderbilt). PeerJ Computer Science (2016), 2015. arXiv:1507.08050 — https://arxiv.org/abs/1507.08050 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1507.08050v1
- **File:** [Salvatier_2015_probabilistic_programming_python_pymc.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Salvatier_2015_probabilistic_programming_python_pymc.pdf) — **20 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** PyMC3 paper: gradient-based MCMC (NUTS) plus ADVI in Python, with trace diagnostics; shows probabilistic programming can carry real applied Bayesian work.
- **Why it matters:** It is the practical on-ramp for GSE's Bayesian stack in the same language as nflverse pipelines.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** GSE engine math; MOVE-37 lab (DeepSeek protocols executed in Python)
- **Second-order uses:** Combine with NumPyro (1912.11554): prototype in PyMC, port hot models to JAX for vectorized posterior predictive checks.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Any Python data-science team doing Bayesian modeling; not sports-bound.
- **Verdict:** ADAPT — **First use:** Re-express one existing rating model in PyMC and diff its posteriors against the current implementation.

### 9. Composable Effects for Flexible and Accelerated Probabilistic Programming in NumPyro
- **Citation:** Du Phan, Neeraj Pradhan, Martin Jankowiak — POSTECH and Uber AI. arXiv preprint (NeurIPS 2019 workshop lineage), 2019. arXiv:1912.11554 — https://arxiv.org/abs/1912.11554 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1912.11554v1
- **File:** [Phan_2019_composable_effects_flexible_accelerated_probabilistic.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Phan_2019_composable_effects_flexible_accelerated_probabilistic.pdf) — **10 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** NumPyro: probabilistic programming on JAX/NumPy with effect handlers, giving GPU/TPU-accelerated inference (NUTS, SVI) with composable model code.
- **Why it matters:** Posterior predictive simulation at slate scale is GSE's compute bottleneck; JAX vectorization is the direct answer.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** GSE engine math; signal pack simulation jobs; fleet automation (GPU batch lanes)
- **Second-order uses:** NumPyro + Fourier Neural Operator surrogate (2010.08895): fast posterior sampling over learned physics/trajectory operators.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Scientific ML generally — climate, fluids, finance time series.
- **Verdict:** ADOPT — **First use:** Port the dynamic Bradley-Terry fit (2003.00083) to NumPyro and time it against the current fit.

### 10. Pyro: Deep Universal Probabilistic Programming
- **Citation:** Eli Bingham, Jonathan P. Chen, Martin Jankowiak, Fritz Obermeyer, Neeraj Pradhan, Theofanis Karaletsos, Rohit Singh, Paul Szerlip, Paul Horsfall, Noah D. Goodman — Uber AI Labs and Stanford University. Journal of Machine Learning Research (2019), 2018. arXiv:1810.09538 — https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.09538 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1810.09538v1
- **File:** [Bingham_2018_pyro_deep_universal_probabilistic_programming.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Bingham_2018_pyro_deep_universal_probabilistic_programming.pdf) — **6 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** Pyro (Uber AI + Stanford): deep universal probabilistic programming — stochastic variational inference that mixes neural nets with probabilistic models, published via JMLR.
- **Why it matters:** GSE has hybrid problems (tracking embeddings + probabilistic heads) that pure Stan-style models express badly; Pyro is built for exactly that seam.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** GSE engine math (tracking-data models); content OS experimentation
- **Second-order uses:** Pyro's SVI with conformal wrappers (2202.13415) gives scalable posteriors plus distribution-free coverage on top.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Industry probabilistic ML at large; robotics, recommendations, forecasting.
- **Verdict:** LEARN-ONLY — **First use:** Write a one-page seam analysis: which current GSE models are hybrid NN+probabilistic and what Pyro would replace.

### 11. The No-U-Turn Sampler: Adaptively Setting Path Lengths in Hamiltonian Monte Carlo
- **Citation:** Matthew D. Hoffman, Andrew Gelman — Columbia University, Department of Statistics. Journal of Machine Learning Research (2014), 2011. arXiv:1111.4246 — https://arxiv.org/abs/1111.4246 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1111.4246v1
- **File:** [Hoffman_2011_no_u_turn_sampler_adaptively.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Hoffman_2011_no_u_turn_sampler_adaptively.pdf) — **30 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** The No-U-Turn Sampler removes HMC's path-length tuning by adaptively choosing trajectory length, with primal-dual step-size adaptation — the sampler that made modern Bayesian workflows routine (Stan's engine).
- **Why it matters:** Every hierarchical rating and injury model GSE runs depends on trustworthy MCMC; NUTS is the reference implementation target and diagnostic baseline.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** GSE engine math; calibration lane
- **Second-order uses:** NUTS posteriors feed conformal calibration (2106.00170) so intervals inherit both Bayesian and distribution-free checks.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Universal: the default sampler across the Bayesian sciences.
- **Verdict:** ADOPT — **First use:** Adopt NUTS diagnostics (divergences, tree depth, E-BFMI) as required fields in every GSE Bayesian fit report.

## 3 — Calibration and distribution-free uncertainty

### 12. Conformal Prediction Under Covariate Shift
- **Citation:** Ryan J. Tibshirani, Rina Foygel Barber, Emmanuel J. Candes, Aaditya Ramdas — Carnegie Mellon, U Chicago, Stanford, CMU (Tibshirani/Barber/Candès/Ramdas). NeurIPS (2019), 2019. arXiv:1904.06019 — https://arxiv.org/abs/1904.06019 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1904.06019v3
- **File:** [Tibshirani_2019_conformal_prediction_under_covariate_shift.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Tibshirani_2019_conformal_prediction_under_covariate_shift.pdf) — **17 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** Weighted conformal prediction restores finite-sample coverage under covariate shift, replacing exchangeability with a weighted notion tied to likelihood ratios.
- **Why it matters:** Sports data shifts constantly — rule changes, pace eras, roster churn. GSE's intervals are only honest if they survive shift; this is the theorem for that.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** Calibration lane; GSE engine (WP/EPA interval outputs)
- **Second-order uses:** Stack with adaptive conformal (2106.00170) for shift that is both covariate-driven and temporal.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Any deployed ML system facing distribution shift: medicine, credit, demand forecasting.
- **Verdict:** ADOPT — **First use:** Implement weighted conformal on one WP model with season as the shift variable; measure coverage by season.

### 13. Distribution-Free Predictive Inference For Regression
- **Citation:** Jing Lei, Max G'Sell, Alessandro Rinaldo, Ryan J. Tibshirani, Larry Wasserman — Carnegie Mellon University, Department of Statistics. Journal of the American Statistical Association (2018), 2016. arXiv:1604.04173 — https://arxiv.org/abs/1604.04173 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1604.04173v2
- **File:** [Lei_2016_distribution_free_predictive_inference_regression.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Lei_2016_distribution_free_predictive_inference_regression.pdf) — **50 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** Lei et al. (CMU Statistics): distribution-free predictive inference for regression — conformal and jackknife-style intervals with finite-sample guarantees and an accuracy/efficiency tradeoff analysis (JASA-class work).
- **Why it matters:** It is the rigorous foundation under every interval GSE publishes; the tradeoff analysis tells us which interval method to use at slate scale.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** Calibration lane; grading lane (postgame interval audits)
- **Second-order uses:** Use its efficiency comparisons to choose the default interval engine inside the conformal stack (1904.06019, 2202.13415).
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Generic regression UQ across science and industry.
- **Verdict:** ADOPT — **First use:** Add this paper's split-conformal baseline as the reference implementation in the calibration test suite.

### 14. Adaptive Conformal Inference Under Distribution Shift
- **Citation:** Isaac Gibbs, Emmanuel Candès — Stanford University (Gibbs & Candès). NeurIPS (2021), 2021. arXiv:2106.00170 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2106.00170 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2106.00170v3
- **File:** [Gibbs_2021_adaptive_conformal_inference_under_distribution.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Gibbs_2021_adaptive_conformal_inference_under_distribution.pdf) — **25 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** Adaptive Conformal Inference (Gibbs & Candès, Stanford): online updating of conformal quantiles that keeps long-run coverage under arbitrary distribution drift, with black-box predictors.
- **Why it matters:** In-season NFL distributions drift weekly. ACI is the conformal method designed for a live, updating engine rather than a static dataset.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** Calibration lane (live in-season updates); GSE engine
- **Second-order uses:** ACI over Bayesian posterior predictives (NumPyro) gives a doubly-defensible interval: model-based center, distribution-free width.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Streaming prediction anywhere: energy load, finance, epidemiology nowcasting.
- **Verdict:** ADOPT — **First use:** Prototype ACI on week-by-week WP residuals from last season's frozen predictions; plot coverage by week.

### 15. Conformal prediction beyond exchangeability
- **Citation:** Rina Foygel Barber, Emmanuel J. Candes, Aaditya Ramdas, Ryan J. Tibshirani — University of Chicago, Stanford, Carnegie Mellon. arXiv preprint; journal version in The Annals of Statistics, 2022. arXiv:2202.13415 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2202.13415 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2202.13415v5
- **File:** [Barber_2022_conformal_prediction_beyond_exchangeability.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Barber_2022_conformal_prediction_beyond_exchangeability.pdf) — **63 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** Barber, Candès, Ramdas & Tibshirani unify conformal beyond exchangeability: robustness bounds showing coverage degrades gracefully under dependence and shift, covering time-series-like data.
- **Why it matters:** Game sequences are dependent, not exchangeable — this paper is the license to use conformal on season-ordered sports data with eyes open about the slack.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** Calibration lane; signal pack validation
- **Second-order uses:** Its robustness bounds quantify exactly how much coverage the ACI layer (2106.00170) must buy back under dependence.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Time-series UQ everywhere; a theory paper with direct engineering content.
- **Verdict:** ADOPT — **First use:** Compute the paper's dependence slack for GSE's weekly WP series and record it in the calibration ledger.

## 4 — Rating systems and doctoral sports statistics

### 16. Asymptotic comparison of identifying constraints for Bradley-Terry models
- **Citation:** Weichen Wu, Brian W. Junker, Nynke M. D. Niezink — Carnegie Mellon University, Statistics & Data Science. Statistics & Probability Letters (as printed on the manuscript), 2022. arXiv:2205.04341 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.04341 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2205.04341v1
- **File:** [Wu_2022_asymptotic_comparison_identifying_constraints_bradley.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Wu_2022_asymptotic_comparison_identifying_constraints_bradley.pdf) — **12 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** For Bradley-Terry models, the choice of identifying constraint (sum-to-zero vs reference-category) changes asymptotic efficiency of pairwise comparisons — constraint choice is not cosmetic.
- **Why it matters:** GSE fits BT-family ratings constantly; this CMU paper says the parameterization itself moves estimator variance, which moves rating stability week to week.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** GSE engine math (ratings); calibration lane
- **Second-order uses:** Combine with dynamic BT estimation (2003.00083): efficient constraints inside a time-varying fit compound the stability gain.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Any paired-comparison system: chess, LLM arenas, consumer preference panels.
- **Verdict:** ADAPT — **First use:** Re-run one existing BT fit under both constraints and compare rating standard errors.

### 17. Efficient Bayesian Inference for Generalized Bradley-Terry Models
- **Citation:** Francois Caron, Arnaud Doucet — INRIA Bordeaux – Sud-Ouest (research report RR-7445); Doucet (Oxford lineage). INRIA Research Report RR-7445 (2010), 2010. arXiv:1011.1761 — https://arxiv.org/abs/1011.1761 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1011.1761v1
- **File:** [Caron_2010_efficient_bayesian_inference_generalized_bradley.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Caron_2010_efficient_bayesian_inference_generalized_bradley.pdf) — **24 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** Caron & Doucet (INRIA/Oxford-lineage): efficient Bayesian inference for generalized Bradley-Terry models via auxiliary-variable Gibbs sampling, covering ties and home advantage extensions (INRIA research report RR-7445).
- **Why it matters:** A principled Bayesian BT with the exact extensions sports ratings need (ties, home edge) and a sampler designed for scale.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** GSE engine math (ratings core)
- **Second-order uses:** Its auxiliary-variable trick ports to the covariate-adjusted BT setting (and informs priors for 2003.00083's dynamic version).
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Paired-comparison inference in psychometrics, marketing, and LLM evaluation.
- **Verdict:** ADAPT — **First use:** Implement the Gibbs sampler on historical NFL game outcomes; compare posterior calibration vs the current point-estimate BT.

### 18. Nonparametric Estimation in the Dynamic Bradley-Terry Model
- **Citation:** Heejong Bong, Wanshan Li, Shamindra Shrotriya, Alessandro Rinaldo — Carnegie Mellon University, Statistics & Data Science. arXiv preprint (CMU Statistics), 2020. arXiv:2003.00083 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2003.00083 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2003.00083v1
- **File:** [Bong_2020_nonparametric_estimation_dynamic_bradley_terry.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Bong_2020_nonparametric_estimation_dynamic_bradley_terry.pdf) — **18 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** Bong, Li, Shrotriya & Rinaldo (CMU): nonparametric estimation for dynamic Bradley-Terry models — time-varying strengths with minimax-rate theory and a practical estimator.
- **Why it matters:** Team strength is not static across a season; this is the statistically honest way to let ratings move, with error guarantees instead of vibes.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** GSE engine math (in-season ratings); signal pack
- **Second-order uses:** Dynamic BT posteriors become features for WP models and for the conformal layer's shift detection (2106.00170).
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Any evolving-strength competition: esports, tennis circuits, sales-team rankings.
- **Verdict:** ADOPT — **First use:** Fit dynamic BT on three NFL seasons and check whether late-season ratings predict playoff outcomes better than static BT.

### 19. A multilevel model with heterogeneous variances for snap timing in the National Football League
- **Citation:** Quang Nguyen, Ronald Yurko — Carnegie Mellon University, Statistics & Data Science (Nguyen & Yurko). arXiv stat.AP (2025), 2025. arXiv:2502.16313 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.16313 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.16313v1
- **File:** [Nguyen_2025_multilevel_model_heterogeneous_variances_snap.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Nguyen_2025_multilevel_model_heterogeneous_variances_snap.pdf) — **24 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** Nguyen & Yurko (CMU Statistics): a multilevel model with heterogeneous variances for NFL snap timing — variance itself is modeled by unit/context, not treated as noise floor (stat.AP, 2025).
- **Why it matters:** Snap timing is a real, chartable NFL signal (cadence, false-start leverage, defensive jump timing) and almost nobody models its variance structure; this is a doctoral-grade, directly-on-target find.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** GSE engine (situational features); signal pack candidate
- **Second-order uses:** Heterogeneous-variance thinking transfers to kicker/punter timing, release-time distributions (RYOE family), and QB cadence-disruption features.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Mostly sports-bound in application, but the heterogeneous-variance multilevel template generalizes to any grouped timing data (manufacturing cycle times, surgical durations).
- **Verdict:** ADAPT — **First use:** Pull snap-timing proxies from play-by-play timestamps and test whether team-level variance clusters persist across seasons.

### 20. Route Identification in the National Football League
- **Citation:** Dani Chu, Matthew Reyers, James Thomson, Lucas Wu — Simon Fraser University, Statistics & Actuarial Science. arXiv preprint (Simon Fraser); journal version in JQAS lineage, 2019. arXiv:1908.02423 — https://arxiv.org/abs/1908.02423 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1908.02423v1 · DOI: 10.1515/jqas-2019-0047
- **File:** [Chu_2019_route_identification_national_football_league.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Chu_2019_route_identification_national_football_league.pdf) — **19 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** Chu et al. (Simon Fraser Statistics): model-based route clustering / route identification in the NFL from tracking data using Gaussian-mixture methodology — turning raw trajectories into named route concepts.
- **Why it matters:** GSE's NGS-replacement spec needs exactly this: unsupervised structure discovery on tracking data that produces human-nameable football objects.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** GSE engine (tracking features); NGS-replacement lane
- **Second-order uses:** Route clusters become categorical features for coverage-matchup models and for SINDy-style dynamics on receiver trajectories (1509.03580).
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Trajectory clustering generalizes to logistics, animal movement, and surveillance analytics; the football instantiation is sports-specific.
- **Verdict:** ADAPT — **First use:** Reproduce the clustering on one public tracking week (Big Data Bowl sample) and validate cluster labels against charted routes.

## 5 — Deep learning: theory, training, scaling, efficiency

### 21. Deep Double Descent: Where Bigger Models and More Data Hurt
- **Citation:** Preetum Nakkiran, Gal Kaplun, Yamini Bansal, Tristan Yang, Boaz Barak, Ilya Sutskever — Harvard University and OpenAI. arXiv preprint; ICLR (2020) version, 2019. arXiv:1912.02292 — https://arxiv.org/abs/1912.02292 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1912.02292v1
- **File:** [Nakkiran_2019_deep_double_descent_where_bigger.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Nakkiran_2019_deep_double_descent_where_bigger.pdf) — **24 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** Deep double descent (Harvard/OpenAI authors): test risk can fall, rise, then fall again as model size or training time grows — the classical U-curve is incomplete, and effective model complexity is the right axis.
- **Why it matters:** It disciplines GSE's model-size decisions: a bigger tracking model that looks worse mid-training may be on the second descent, not failing.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** GSE engine (model selection doctrine); fleet model training
- **Second-order uses:** Read against scaling laws (2001.08361, 2203.15556): double descent explains the noise, scaling laws give the budget curve.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** All of deep learning practice; a conceptual correction with global reach.
- **Verdict:** LEARN-ONLY — **First use:** Add an effective-complexity note to the model registry template so capacity decisions cite this instead of parameter counts alone.

### 22. Understanding deep learning requires rethinking generalization
- **Citation:** Chiyuan Zhang, Samy Bengio, Moritz Hardt, Benjamin Recht, Oriol Vinyals — MIT, UC Berkeley, Google Brain/DeepMind. ICLR (2017), 2016. arXiv:1611.03530 — https://arxiv.org/abs/1611.03530 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1611.03530v2
- **File:** [Zhang_2016_understanding_deep_learning_requires_rethinking.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Zhang_2016_understanding_deep_learning_requires_rethinking.pdf) — **15 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** Zhang et al. (MIT/Berkeley/Google): standard nets fit random labels, so classical generalization theory cannot explain why they generalize — explicit regularization is not the whole story.
- **Why it matters:** A permanent warning label for GSE's ML work: training fit proves nothing; only held-out, leakage-controlled evaluation counts. This is the theory behind the house 'measured, not guessed' rule.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** GSE engine evaluation doctrine; fleet/automation (eval gates)
- **Second-order uses:** Pairs with HELM (2211.09110) and LLM-judge audits (2306.05685) to form the fleet's evaluation stack: fit is not evidence, benchmarks are audited, judges are biased measurably.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Universal ML methodology.
- **Verdict:** ADOPT — **First use:** Cite it in the engine's evaluation checklist as the reason random-label sanity checks run on any new feature pipeline.

### 23. Neural Tangent Kernel: Convergence and Generalization in Neural Networks
- **Citation:** Arthur Jacot, Franck Gabriel, Clément Hongler — EPFL and Imperial College London. NeurIPS (2018), 2018. arXiv:1806.07572 — https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.07572 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1806.07572v4
- **File:** [Jacot_2018_neural_tangent_kernel_convergence_generalization.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Jacot_2018_neural_tangent_kernel_convergence_generalization.pdf) — **19 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** Neural Tangent Kernel (Jacot, Gabriel, Hongler): infinitely wide networks evolve under a fixed kernel during gradient training, making deep learning analyzable as kernel regression (NeurIPS 2018).
- **Why it matters:** NTK gives GSE a closed-form lens for small-data regimes — exactly where sports models live — and a principled way to reason about training dynamics instead of folklore.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** GSE engine math (small-sample model theory); MOVE-37 framework atlas (kernel methods)
- **Second-order uses:** NTK + Gaussian-process thinking bridges to the rating/state-space stack; also a MOVE-37 'framework' with a concrete sports test (kernel ridge on team features).
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Core theory for all of ML; also informs neural-operator analysis.
- **Verdict:** LEARN-ONLY — **First use:** Run kernel-ridge with an NTK on one tabular GSE task and compare to the gradient-boosted baseline.

### 24. The Lottery Ticket Hypothesis: Finding Sparse, Trainable Neural Networks
- **Citation:** Jonathan Frankle, Michael Carbin — MIT CSAIL. ICLR (2019), 2018. arXiv:1803.03635 — https://arxiv.org/abs/1803.03635 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1803.03635v5
- **File:** [Frankle_2018_lottery_ticket_hypothesis_finding_sparse.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Frankle_2018_lottery_ticket_hypothesis_finding_sparse.pdf) — **42 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** Lottery Ticket Hypothesis (Frankle & Carbin, MIT CSAIL; ICLR 2019): dense networks contain sparse subnetworks that train to full accuracy — initialization, not just architecture, carries the win.
- **Why it matters:** Sparse winning tickets mean cheaper inference for the fleet's always-on models and a research thread on which subnetworks encode sports structure.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** Fleet/automation (inference cost); GSE engine (model compression)
- **Second-order uses:** With LoRA (2106.09685): tickets for base models, adapters for tasks — a two-level efficiency stack for the content OS's model fleet.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Edge deployment everywhere; green-AI accounting.
- **Verdict:** LEARN-ONLY — **First use:** Try iterative magnitude pruning on one GSE tabular net and record the accuracy/sparsity frontier.

### 25. Adam: A Method for Stochastic Optimization
- **Citation:** Diederik P. Kingma, Jimmy Ba — University of Amsterdam and University of Toronto. ICLR (2015), 2014. arXiv:1412.6980 — https://arxiv.org/abs/1412.6980 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1412.6980v9
- **File:** [Kingma_2014_adam_method_stochastic_optimization.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Kingma_2014_adam_method_stochastic_optimization.pdf) — **15 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** Adam (Kingma & Ba): adaptive per-parameter learning rates from first/second moment estimates — the optimizer that became the default for training neural networks (ICLR 2015).
- **Why it matters:** Baseline literacy for every training run in the fleet; also a reminder that optimizer choice is a tuned hyperparameter, not a constant.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** Fleet model training; GSE engine NN components
- **Second-order uses:** Adam's moment estimates motivate the adaptive-update thinking behind ASHA/BOHB scheduling (1810.05934, 1807.01774).
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Universal.
- **Verdict:** LEARN-ONLY — **First use:** No build; file as the citation of record when optimizer defaults are documented in training configs.

- **File:** [He_2015_deep_residual_learning_image_recognition.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/He_2015_deep_residual_learning_image_recognition.pdf) — **12 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
