### 27. Attention Is All You Need
- **Citation:** Ashish Vaswani, Noam Shazeer, Niki Parmar, Jakob Uszkoreit, Llion Jones, Aidan N. Gomez, Lukasz Kaiser, Illia Polosukhin — Google Brain / Google Research. NeurIPS (2017), 2017. arXiv:1706.03762 — https://arxiv.org/abs/1706.03762 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1706.03762v7
- **File:** [Vaswani_2017_attention_is_all_you_need.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Vaswani_2017_attention_is_all_you_need.pdf) — **15 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** Attention Is All You Need (Google): the Transformer — self-attention only, no recurrence — with state-of-the-art translation results and the architecture every current LLM descends from.
- **Why it matters:** Foundational provenance for the entire agent fleet: every model Garrett rents reasons through this architecture; understanding it is understanding the fleet's substrate.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** Fleet/automation (model literacy); content OS
- **Second-order uses:** Attention maps over play sequences are a candidate explainability surface for tracking models (cf. factorized attention work already in his corpus).
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Universal; arguably the most consequential ML paper of the decade.
- **Verdict:** LEARN-ONLY — **First use:** File as substrate citation in the fleet's model-routing documentation.

### 28. Scaling Laws for Neural Language Models
- **Citation:** Jared Kaplan, Sam McCandlish, Tom Henighan, Tom B. Brown, Benjamin Chess, Rewon Child, Scott Gray, Alec Radford, Jeffrey Wu, Dario Amodei — OpenAI and Johns Hopkins University. OpenAI technical report (arXiv), 2020. arXiv:2001.08361 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2001.08361 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2001.08361v1
- **File:** [Kaplan_2020_scaling_laws_neural_language_models.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Kaplan_2020_scaling_laws_neural_language_models.pdf) — **30 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** Scaling Laws (Kaplan et al., OpenAI/JHU): loss follows smooth power laws in parameters, data, and compute, largely independent of architecture details — enabling compute budgeting before training.
- **Why it matters:** The fleet rents and routes models by cost/quality; scaling laws are the economics underneath every routing decision and fine-tune budget.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** Fleet/automation (model routing economics); content OS
- **Second-order uses:** With Chinchilla (2203.15556) it brackets the training-budget question from both eras; with double descent (1912.02292) it explains when the curve kinks.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** AI economics globally; also a template for any empirical power-law system.
- **Verdict:** LEARN-ONLY — **First use:** Add a scaling-law note to the routing doc: expected quality deltas per dollar across the fleet's current model menu.

### 29. Training Compute-Optimal Large Language Models
- **Citation:** Jordan Hoffmann, Sebastian Borgeaud, Arthur Mensch, Elena Buchatskaya, Trevor Cai, Eliza Rutherford, Diego de Las Casas, Lisa Anne Hendricks, Johannes Welbl, Aidan Clark, Tom Hennigan, Eric Noland, Katie Millican, George van den Driessche, Bogdan Damoc, Aurelia Guy, Simon Osindero, Karen Simonyan, Erich Elsen, Jack W. Rae, Oriol Vinyals, Laurent Sifre — DeepMind. DeepMind technical report (arXiv), 2022. arXiv:2203.15556 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.15556 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2203.15556v1
- **File:** [Hoffmann_2022_training_compute_optimal_large_language.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Hoffmann_2022_training_compute_optimal_large_language.pdf) — **36 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** Chinchilla (DeepMind): compute-optimal training balances parameters and tokens (~20 tokens/parameter); most large models of the era were undertrained — a 70B model beat 280B Gopher on the same budget.
- **Why it matters:** Directly actionable for any fine-tune Garrett funds (e.g., Nova/persona or sports-domain adapters): data quantity, not just model size, is the lever.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** Fleet model training budgets; persona/content model work
- **Second-order uses:** LoRA (2106.09685) on a Chinchilla-proportioned corpus is the realistic small-shop version of this lesson.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Every organization's AI budget math.
- **Verdict:** ADAPT — **First use:** When the next fine-tune is scoped, require a tokens-per-parameter line in the plan citing this paper.

### 30. LoRA: Low-Rank Adaptation of Large Language Models
- **Citation:** Edward J. Hu, Yelong Shen, Phillip Wallis, Zeyuan Allen-Zhu, Yuanzhi Li, Shean Wang, Lu Wang, Weizhu Chen — Microsoft. ICLR (2022), 2021. arXiv:2106.09685 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2106.09685 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2106.09685v2
- **File:** [Hu_2021_lora_low_rank_adaptation_large.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Hu_2021_lora_low_rank_adaptation_large.pdf) — **26 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** LoRA (Microsoft): freeze the base model, train low-rank adapter matrices — matching full fine-tuning quality at a fraction of parameters, storage, and switching cost (ICLR 2022).
- **Why it matters:** This is how a one-person shop maintains many specialized models: one base, many cheap adapters — personas, sports-domain tunings, client variants.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** Content OS (Nova/persona adapters); fleet model customization
- **Second-order uses:** LoRA adapters + the routing layer: adapters become addressable skills in the fleet's model menu, swappable per task.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Universal fine-tuning economics.
- **Verdict:** ADOPT — **First use:** Inventory current fine-tune artifacts and mark which should have been LoRA adapters; set LoRA as default for the next one.

## 6 — Preference learning, reasoning, and evaluation

### 31. Training language models to follow instructions with human feedback
- **Citation:** Long Ouyang, Jeff Wu, Xu Jiang, Diogo Almeida, Carroll L. Wainwright, Pamela Mishkin, Chong Zhang, Sandhini Agarwal, Katarina Slama, Alex Ray, John Schulman, Jacob Hilton, Fraser Kelton, Luke Miller, Maddie Simens, Amanda Askell, Peter Welinder, Paul Christiano, Jan Leike, Ryan Lowe — OpenAI. NeurIPS (2022), 2022. arXiv:2203.02155 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.02155 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2203.02155v1
- **File:** [Ouyang_2022_training_language_models_follow_instructions.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Ouyang_2022_training_language_models_follow_instructions.pdf) — **68 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** InstructGPT (OpenAI): RLHF — SFT plus a reward model trained on human rankings plus PPO — makes smaller models outperform much larger raw models on human preference.
- **Why it matters:** The canonical preference-learning pipeline; GSE's own pick-grading and content-taste problems are preference problems wearing different clothes.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** Fleet/automation (preference data pipelines); content OS quality ranking
- **Second-order uses:** DPO (2305.18290) is the simplification of this pipeline; read together they bracket the design space for any future 'grade like Garrett' model.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** All human-feedback ML; product ranking generally.
- **Verdict:** LEARN-ONLY — **First use:** Map the pipeline onto GSE's settled-pick data: what would the reward model be, and what labels already exist?

### 32. Chain-of-Thought Prompting Elicits Reasoning in Large Language Models
- **Citation:** Jason Wei, Xuezhi Wang, Dale Schuurmans, Maarten Bosma, Brian Ichter, Fei Xia, Ed Chi, Quoc Le, Denny Zhou — Google Research, Brain Team. NeurIPS (2022), 2022. arXiv:2201.11903 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2201.11903 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2201.11903v6
- **File:** [Wei_2022_chain_thought_prompting_elicits_reasoning.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Wei_2022_chain_thought_prompting_elicits_reasoning.pdf) — **43 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** Chain-of-Thought (Google Brain): eliciting explicit intermediate reasoning steps sharply improves multi-step problem solving in large models, with gains emerging at scale.
- **Why it matters:** The fleet's analyst lanes run on structured reasoning; CoT is the primary-source justification for the house style of showing work — and its limits (faithfulness) are the caution.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** Fleet/automation (agent prompting standards); GSE reasoning trails
- **Second-order uses:** CoT traces + Reflexion (2303.11366) memory: reasoning that is stored, scored, and reused becomes a training asset, not exhaust.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** All LLM application design.
- **Verdict:** LEARN-ONLY — **First use:** Reference it in the agent-brief template where step-by-step reasoning is required, alongside a faithfulness warning.

### 33. Direct Preference Optimization: Your Language Model is Secretly a Reward Model
- **Citation:** Rafael Rafailov, Archit Sharma, Eric Mitchell, Stefano Ermon, Christopher D. Manning, Chelsea Finn — Stanford University and CZ Biohub. NeurIPS (2023), 2023. arXiv:2305.18290 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.18290 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.18290v3
- **File:** [Rafailov_2023_direct_preference_optimization_your_language.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Rafailov_2023_direct_preference_optimization_your_language.pdf) — **27 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** Direct Preference Optimization (Rafailov et al. — Stanford/CZ Biohub): the RLHF objective has a closed-form optimal policy, so preference learning collapses to a simple classification-style loss on preference pairs — no reward model, no RL loop (NeurIPS 2023).
- **Why it matters:** It removes the two most fragile parts of preference training (reward-model drift, PPO instability) — directly relevant if GSE ever trains a 'grades like the house' model on settled outcomes or content preferences.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** Fleet/automation (preference training); content OS quality models
- **Second-order uses:** DPO on top of LoRA adapters (2106.09685) is the cheapest credible preference-tuning stack available to a small shop; its pair data can come from Garrett's own accept/reject history on drafts and picks.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Preference learning for any product with pairwise human judgments — search, recommendations, support quality.
- **Verdict:** ADAPT — **First use:** Inventory existing pairwise preference data in the corpus (accepted vs rejected drafts/posts) and estimate whether a DPO pilot has enough pairs to be honest.

### 34. Judging LLM-as-a-Judge with MT-Bench and Chatbot Arena
- **Citation:** Lianmin Zheng, Wei-Lin Chiang, Ying Sheng, Siyuan Zhuang, Zhanghao Wu, Yonghao Zhuang, Zi Lin, Zhuohan Li, Dacheng Li, Eric P. Xing, Hao Zhang, Joseph E. Gonzalez, Ion Stoica — UC Berkeley, UC San Diego, CMU, Stanford, MBZUAI. NeurIPS Datasets & Benchmarks (2023), 2023. arXiv:2306.05685 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.05685 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2306.05685v4
- **File:** [Zheng_2023_judging_llm_as_judge_mt.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Zheng_2023_judging_llm_as_judge_mt.pdf) — **29 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **STRUCTURE:** Evaluation harness design: paired-comparison judging with bias controls.
- **What it proves/builds:** MT-Bench/Chatbot Arena (Berkeley/UCSD/CMU/Stanford): LLM-as-a-judge agrees with humans ~85% but carries measurable position, verbosity, and self-enhancement biases — judging is an instrument that itself needs calibration.
- **Why it matters:** GSE already lived this with the Laya gate eval: a judge that ratifies is not a judge. This paper is the measurement framework for any future judge seat, including Jev candidates.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** Calibration lane (judge evaluation); fleet QA/QC
- **Second-order uses:** Its bias battery becomes part of the standing judge audition built from the Laya harness — any candidate judge gets bias-tested, not just accuracy-tested.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** LLM evaluation everywhere; also a template for auditing any automated grader.
- **Verdict:** ADOPT — **First use:** Add the three bias checks (position swap, verbosity control, self-preference) to the judge-audition protocol document.

### 35. Holistic Evaluation of Language Models
- **Citation:** Percy Liang, Rishi Bommasani, Tony Lee, Dimitris Tsipras, Dilara Soylu, Michihiro Yasunaga, Yian Zhang, Deepak Narayanan, Yuhuai Wu, Ananya Kumar, Benjamin Newman, Binhang Yuan, Bobby Yan, Ce Zhang, Christian Cosgrove, Christopher D. Manning, Christopher Ré, Diana Acosta-Navas, Drew A. Hudson, Eric Zelikman, Esin Durmus, Faisal Ladhak, Frieda Rong, Hongyu Ren, Huaxiu Yao, Jue Wang, Keshav Santhanam, Laurel Orr, Lucia Zheng, Mert Yuksekgonul, Mirac Suzgun, Nathan Kim, Neel Guha, Niladri Chatterji, Omar Khattab, Peter Henderson, Qian Huang, Ryan Chi, Sang Michael Xie, Shibani Santurkar, Surya Ganguli, Tatsunori Hashimoto, Thomas Icard, Tianyi Zhang, Vishrav Chaudhary, William Wang, Xuechen Li, Yifan Mai, Yuhui Zhang, Yuta Koreeda — Stanford CRFM (and MIT co-authors). Transactions on Machine Learning Research (2023), 2022. arXiv:2211.09110 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.09110 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2211.09110v2
- **File:** [Liang_2022_holistic_evaluation_language_models.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Liang_2022_holistic_evaluation_language_models.pdf) — **162 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **STRUCTURE:** Evaluation harness: scenario × metric matrix with raw-output publication.
- **What it proves/builds:** HELM (Stanford CRFM; TMLR 2023): holistic, transparent evaluation across scenarios and metrics — accuracy alongside calibration, robustness, fairness, efficiency — with standardized prompting and public raw outputs.
- **Why it matters:** It is the design pattern for GSE's own evaluation reporting: many metrics, no cherry-picking, raw artifacts published. Directly matches the house gap-audit instinct.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** Fleet QA/QC; GSE engine evaluation reporting; calibration lane
- **Second-order uses:** HELM's scenario/metric matrix is the template for a 'GSE-HELM' over pick types: accuracy, calibration, CLV, and robustness reported together or not at all.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Benchmark design across AI; a standard others cite.
- **Verdict:** ADAPT — **First use:** Draft the GSE evaluation matrix (scenarios × metrics) modeled on HELM's table structure.

## 7 — Model tuning and hyperparameter optimization

### 36. Practical Bayesian Optimization of Machine Learning Algorithms
- **Citation:** Jasper Snoek, Hugo Larochelle, Ryan P. Adams — University of Toronto, Université de Sherbrooke, Harvard. NeurIPS (2012), 2012. arXiv:1206.2944 — https://arxiv.org/abs/1206.2944 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1206.2944v2
- **File:** [Snoek_2012_practical_bayesian_optimization_machine_learning.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Snoek_2012_practical_bayesian_optimization_machine_learning.pdf) — **12 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** Practical Bayesian Optimization (Snoek, Larochelle, Adams — Toronto/Sherbrooke/Harvard): GP-based BayesOpt with practical kernels and cost modeling beats expert manual tuning on real ML problems (NeurIPS 2012).
- **Why it matters:** The founding practical paper for GSE's tuning lane: hyperparameters of rating and WP models should be searched by a surrogate, not by hand.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** GSE engine (tuning pipeline); calibration lane experiments
- **Second-order uses:** This is the theory layer under BOHB (1807.01774) and Optuna-style tooling; together they define the tuning stack from principle to production.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Experimental optimization in any field — chemistry, robotics, A/B testing.
- **Verdict:** ADOPT — **First use:** Wire a GP-BayesOpt loop for one WP model's hyperparameters with a fixed evaluation budget and log the search trace.

### 37. A Tutorial on Bayesian Optimization
- **Citation:** Peter I. Frazier — Cornell University (Frazier). arXiv tutorial; INFORMS TutORials in Operations Research version (2018), 2018. arXiv:1807.02811 — https://arxiv.org/abs/1807.02811 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1807.02811v1
- **File:** [Frazier_2018_tutorial_bayesian_optimization.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Frazier_2018_tutorial_bayesian_optimization.pdf) — **22 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** Frazier's tutorial (Cornell): a self-contained, rigorous walkthrough of Bayesian optimization — GP surrogates, acquisition functions (EI, UCB, knowledge gradient), and implementation pitfalls.
- **Why it matters:** The teachable reference: any fleet agent (or Garrett) can read this one document and run the tuning lane correctly.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** Fleet knowledge base; GSE tuning lane onboarding
- **Second-order uses:** Tutorial + Snoek (1206.2944) gives both the 'how' and the 'proof it beats experts'; file them as a pair in the tuning runbook.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Universal experimental-design literacy.
- **Verdict:** ADOPT — **First use:** Adopt as the tuning lane's canonical reference; require acquisition-function choice to be stated in every tuning log.

### 38. Hyperband: A Novel Bandit-Based Approach to Hyperparameter Optimization
- **Citation:** Lisha Li, Kevin Jamieson, Giulia DeSalvo, Afshin Rostamizadeh, Ameet Talwalkar — Carnegie Mellon University and Google (JMLR). Journal of Machine Learning Research 18 (2018), 2016. arXiv:1603.06560 — https://arxiv.org/abs/1603.06560 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1603.06560v4
- **File:** [Li_2016_hyperband_novel_bandit_based_approach.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Li_2016_hyperband_novel_bandit_based_approach.pdf) — **52 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** Hyperband (Li, Jamieson, DeSalvo, Rostamizadeh, Talwalkar — CMU/Google): bandit-based early stopping allocates resources to promising configurations, with theory and large speedups (JMLR 2018).
- **Why it matters:** GSE's experiment volume is compute-bound; Hyperband's successive halving is the cheapest large win available to the tuning lane.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** GSE engine experiments; fleet training jobs
- **Second-order uses:** Hyperband is the resource-allocation half of BOHB (1807.01774); its brackets also schedule conformal/ACI evaluation sweeps cheaply.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Any budgeted search: drug screening, simulation studies, product experiments.
- **Verdict:** ADOPT — **First use:** Implement successive halving around the existing model-comparison harness and measure wall-clock saved per experiment cycle.

### 39. BOHB: Robust and Efficient Hyperparameter Optimization at Scale
- **Citation:** Stefan Falkner, Aaron Klein, Frank Hutter — University of Freiburg (Hutter group). ICML (2018), 2018. arXiv:1807.01774 — https://arxiv.org/abs/1807.01774 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1807.01774v1
- **File:** [Falkner_2018_bohb_robust_efficient_hyperparameter_optimization.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Falkner_2018_bohb_robust_efficient_hyperparameter_optimization.pdf) — **19 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** BOHB (Falkner, Klein, Hutter — Freiburg): Hyperband's resource efficiency plus Bayesian optimization's guidance, robust across benchmarks at scale (ICML 2018).
- **Why it matters:** The production-grade answer when GSE tuning has both many configurations and real budgets — the current best default for the tuning lane.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** GSE engine (tuning pipeline)
- **Second-order uses:** BOHB search traces double as a dataset for meta-learning which GSE model families tune easily — a fleet-level asset.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** AutoML practice generally.
- **Verdict:** ADAPT — **First use:** Pilot BOHB on the signal-pack's threshold/parameter search with a capped budget; compare against random search at equal compute.

### 40. A System for Massively Parallel Hyperparameter Tuning
- **Citation:** Liam Li, Kevin Jamieson, Afshin Rostamizadeh, Ekaterina Gonina, Moritz Hardt, Benjamin Recht, Ameet Talwalkar — Carnegie Mellon University and Microsoft. MLSys (2020), 2018. arXiv:1810.05934 — https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.05934 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/1810.05934v5
- **File:** [Li_2018_system_massively_parallel_hyperparameter_tuning.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Li_2018_system_massively_parallel_hyperparameter_tuning.pdf) — **17 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** ASHA (Li et al. — CMU/Microsoft lineage): asynchronous successive halving removes the synchronization bottleneck of parallel tuning, scaling near-linearly with workers (MLSys 2020).
- **Why it matters:** The fleet is inherently parallel and asynchronous; ASHA is the tuning scheduler shaped like the fleet itself.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** Fleet/automation (parallel experiment scheduling); GSE tuning lane
- **Second-order uses:** ASHA's async promotion logic is a design cousin of the fleet's task queues — the same rung-promotion idea can schedule agent work, not just trials.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Distributed experimentation platforms everywhere.
- **Verdict:** ADAPT — **First use:** Sketch an ASHA-style rung ladder for agent task evaluation (promote/kill thresholds) as a fleet ops note.

## 8 — Datasets, solved-problem corpora, and evaluation harnesses (STRUCTURE)

### 41. Training Verifiers to Solve Math Word Problems
- **Citation:** Karl Cobbe, Vineet Kosaraju, Mohammad Bavarian, Mark Chen, Heewoo Jun, Lukasz Kaiser, Matthias Plappert, Jerry Tworek, Jacob Hilton, Reiichiro Nakano, Christopher Hesse, John Schulman — OpenAI. OpenAI technical report (arXiv), 2021. arXiv:2110.14168 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2110.14168 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2110.14168v2
- **File:** [Cobbe_2021_training_verifiers_solve_math_word.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Cobbe_2021_training_verifiers_solve_math_word.pdf) — **22 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **STRUCTURE:** STRUCTURE — solved-problem corpus (8,500 problems with worked solutions and calculator annotations) plus a verifier-training recipe.
- **What it proves/builds:** GSM8K (OpenAI): 8.5K human-written grade-school math problems with natural-language solutions, plus the finding that training verifiers to rank candidate solutions beats fine-tuning generators alone.
- **Why it matters:** Two assets in one: a solved-problem corpus (the class of Garrett's reference find) and the verifier-over-generator result, which is the architectural lesson behind judge/verifier lanes.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** Fleet QA/QC (verifier design); MOVE-37 (worked-solution corpus patterns)
- **Second-order uses:** Verifier training + LeanDojo (2306.15626) retrieval: rank candidate proofs/solutions with a learned verifier instead of trusting the generator — the Laya lesson, done right.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Any generation task with checkable outputs: code, math, structured data.
- **Verdict:** ADOPT — **First use:** Write the verifier-first design note for the next judge audition, citing the GSM8K verifier ablation as evidence.

### 42. Measuring Mathematical Problem Solving With the MATH Dataset
- **Citation:** Dan Hendrycks, Collin Burns, Saurav Kadavath, Akul Arora, Steven Basart, Eric Tang, Dawn Song, Jacob Steinhardt — UC Berkeley and University of Chicago. NeurIPS Datasets & Benchmarks (2021), 2021. arXiv:2103.03874 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2103.03874 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2103.03874v2
- **File:** [Hendrycks_2021_measuring_mathematical_problem_solving_math.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Hendrycks_2021_measuring_mathematical_problem_solving_math.pdf) — **22 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **STRUCTURE:** STRUCTURE — 12,500 problems with full solutions, topic/level taxonomy (prealgebra through precalculus, Levels 1-5).
- **What it proves/builds:** MATH dataset (Hendrycks et al. — Berkeley/UChicago): 12,500 competition problems with full step-by-step solutions, establishing that even strong models initially solved only a small fraction — a durable reasoning yardstick.
- **Why it matters:** A doctoral-grade benchmark whose solutions are worked, not just answered; useful as a reasoning-eval set for the fleet's analyst models and as a corpus-design exemplar.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** Fleet evaluation (reasoning benchmarks); MOVE-37 corpus design
- **Second-order uses:** MATH + GSM8K (2110.14168) bracket difficulty; a routing lane can place any new model on the bracket in one sitting.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** AI evaluation standard; education technology.
- **Verdict:** LEARN-ONLY — **First use:** Add MATH (or its public subsets) to the fleet's standard reasoning spot-check list.

### 43. Evaluating Large Language Models Trained on Code
- **Citation:** Mark Chen, Jerry Tworek, Heewoo Jun, Qiming Yuan, Henrique Ponde de Oliveira Pinto, Jared Kaplan, Harri Edwards, Yuri Burda, Nicholas Joseph, Greg Brockman, Alex Ray, Raul Puri, Gretchen Krueger, Michael Petrov, Heidy Khlaaf, Girish Sastry, Pamela Mishkin, Brooke Chan, Scott Gray, Nick Ryder, Mikhail Pavlov, Alethea Power, Lukasz Kaiser, Mohammad Bavarian, Clemens Winter, Philippe Tillet, Felipe Petroski Such, Dave Cummings, Matthias Plappert, Fotios Chantzis, Elizabeth Barnes, Ariel Herbert-Voss, William Hebgen Guss, Alex Nichol, Alex Paino, Nikolas Tezak, Jie Tang, Igor Babuschkin, Suchir Balaji, Shantanu Jain, William Saunders, Christopher Hesse, Andrew N. Carr, Jan Leike, Josh Achiam, Vedant Misra, Evan Morikawa, Alec Radford, Matthew Knight, Miles Brundage, Mira Murati, Katie Mayer, Peter Welinder, Bob McGrew, Dario Amodei, Sam McCandlish, Ilya Sutskever, Wojciech Zaremba — OpenAI. OpenAI technical report (arXiv), 2021. arXiv:2107.03374 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2107.03374 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2107.03374v2
- **File:** [Chen_2021_evaluating_large_language_models_trained.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Chen_2021_evaluating_large_language_models_trained.pdf) — **35 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **STRUCTURE:** STRUCTURE — benchmark harness: problems + unit tests + pass@k estimator.
- **What it proves/builds:** HumanEval (OpenAI): 164 hand-written programming problems with unit tests, introducing pass@k as the functional-correctness metric for code models.
- **Why it matters:** pass@k thinking is how the fleet should score its coding agents (Jules, DeepSeek lanes): samples, tests, and a probability of at least one pass — not vibes.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** Fleet QA/QC (coding-agent scorecards); Jules lane evaluation
- **Second-order uses:** HumanEval's harness pattern generalizes into GSE's own task suites: every recurring agent task gets tests and a pass@k.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Code-model evaluation standard worldwide.
- **Verdict:** ADAPT — **First use:** Adopt pass@k language in the Jules QC scorecard for multi-attempt tasks.

### 44. SWE-bench: Can Language Models Resolve Real-World GitHub Issues?
- **Citation:** Carlos E. Jimenez, John Yang, Alexander Wettig, Shunyu Yao, Kexin Pei, Ofir Press, Karthik Narasimhan — Princeton University and University of Chicago. ICLR (2024), 2023. arXiv:2310.06770 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.06770 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.06770v3
- **File:** [Jimenez_2023_swe_bench_can_language_models.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Jimenez_2023_swe_bench_can_language_models.pdf) — **52 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **STRUCTURE:** STRUCTURE — 2,294-task benchmark with executable grading (FAIL_TO_PASS / PASS_TO_PASS test sets).
- **What it proves/builds:** SWE-bench (Princeton/Chicago): 2,294 real GitHub issue-resolution tasks from 12 Python repos, graded by the repos' own test suites — the benchmark that made agent coding claims falsifiable (ICLR 2024).
- **Why it matters:** It is the standing reality check for every coding-agent claim in the fleet, and its construction (real issues, real tests) is the template for a GSE-internal bench.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** Fleet QA/QC; Jules/DeepSeek lane evaluation; SWE-agent comparisons
- **Second-order uses:** SWE-bench + SWE-agent (2405.15793): benchmark and agent from overlapping Princeton circles — run the agent on the bench's lite split for a local capability read.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Software-engineering automation evaluation, industry-wide.
- **Verdict:** ADOPT — **First use:** Stand up SWE-bench Lite locally and baseline the fleet's current coding route before the next big build wave.

### 45. MiniF2F: a cross-system benchmark for formal Olympiad-level mathematics
- **Citation:** Kunhao Zheng, Jesse Michael Han, Stanislas Polu — École Polytechnique, OpenAI, University of Pittsburgh. ICLR (2022), 2021. arXiv:2109.00110 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2109.00110 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2109.00110v2
- **File:** [Zheng_2021_minif2f_cross_system_benchmark_formal.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Zheng_2021_minif2f_cross_system_benchmark_formal.pdf) — **11 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **STRUCTURE:** STRUCTURE — 488 formalized problems across three proof systems with a shared statement set.
- **What it proves/builds:** MiniF2F (Zheng, Han et al. — École Polytechnique/OpenAI): a cross-system benchmark (Lean, Isabelle, Metamath) of 488 formalized Olympiad problems for neural theorem proving (ICLR 2022).
- **Why it matters:** Formal benchmarks are where 'solved' is machine-checked, not argued — the standard Garrett's reference find (openai/math) is being judged against, and a template for verifiable corpora.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** MOVE-37 (verification standards); fleet QA/QC
- **Second-order uses:** MiniF2F + LeanDojo (2306.15626): benchmark plus environment — together a complete theorem-proving evaluation lane.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Formal-methods and AI-for-math research generally.
- **Verdict:** LEARN-ONLY — **First use:** Cite MiniF2F's cross-system design in the MOVE-37 verification note: claims should ship with a checker.

### 46. LeanDojo: Theorem Proving with Retrieval-Augmented Language Models
- **Citation:** Kaiyu Yang, Aidan M. Swope, Alex Gu, Rahul Chalamala, Peiyang Song, Shixing Yu, Saad Godil, Ryan Prenger, Anima Anandkumar — Caltech, NVIDIA, MIT, UC Santa Barbara, UT Austin. NeurIPS Datasets & Benchmarks (2023), 2023. arXiv:2306.15626 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.15626 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2306.15626v2
- **File:** [Yang_2023_leandojo_theorem_proving_retrieval_augmented.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Yang_2023_leandojo_theorem_proving_retrieval_augmented.pdf) — **40 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **STRUCTURE:** STRUCTURE — theorem corpus + interactive environment + retrieval index + benchmark split.
- **What it proves/builds:** LeanDojo (Yang et al. — Caltech/NVIDIA/MIT/UCSB/UT Austin): an open environment and dataset (98K+ theorems from mathlib) for retrieval-augmented theorem proving, with the ReProver model (NeurIPS 2023 Datasets & Benchmarks).
- **Why it matters:** It turns theorem proving into an agent environment with retrieval — the working machinery behind machine-checked mathematics, directly relevant to MOVE-37's verification ambitions.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** MOVE-37; fleet/automation (agent environments)
- **Second-order uses:** LeanDojo's retrieval-over-lemmas pattern mirrors GSE's retrieval-over-corpus design for research agents — same architecture, different library.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** AI-for-math infrastructure; formal verification in software.
- **Verdict:** ADAPT — **First use:** Install LeanDojo in a scratch env and run ReProver on a handful of mathlib theorems to learn the environment's shape.

### 47. FrontierMath: A Benchmark for Evaluating Advanced Mathematical Reasoning in AI
- **Citation:** Elliot Glazer, Ege Erdil, Tamay Besiroglu, Diego Chicharro, Evan Chen, Alex Gunning, Caroline Falkman Olsson, Jean-Stanislas Denain, Anson Ho, Emily de Oliveira Santos, Olli Järviniemi, Matthew Barnett, Robert Sandler, Matej Vrzala, Jaime Sevilla, Qiuyu Ren, Elizabeth Pratt, Lionel Levine, Grant Barkley, Natalie Stewart, Bogdan Grechuk, Tetiana Grechuk, Shreepranav Varma Enugandla, Mark Wildon — Epoch AI, with 60+ contributing research mathematicians. Epoch AI report (arXiv, 2024), 2024. arXiv:2411.04872 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.04872 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2411.04872v7
- **File:** [Glazer_2024_frontiermath_benchmark_evaluating_advanced_mathematical.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Glazer_2024_frontiermath_benchmark_evaluating_advanced_mathematical.pdf) — **28 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **STRUCTURE:** STRUCTURE — 350 unpublished research problems with programmatic answer verification and expert provenance.
- **What it proves/builds:** FrontierMath (Epoch AI, with 60+ research mathematicians incl. Fields medalists): 350 original, unpublished, research-level problems with automated verification — built specifically so scores cannot be gamed by training on the test (arXiv 2024; later expanded).
- **Why it matters:** This is the current gold standard for 'is the model actually doing mathematics' and the closest published cousin of the evaluation culture behind Garrett's OpenAI reference find.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** MOVE-37 (ambition calibration); fleet evaluation
- **Second-order uses:** FrontierMath's unpublished-problem discipline is the anti-contamination template for GSE's own holdout design (sealed sets, like the Laya frozen set).
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Frontier AI evaluation policy; research-integrity methodology.
- **Verdict:** LEARN-ONLY — **First use:** Record FrontierMath's design rules (original problems, expert review, automated checking) in the holdout-design note for future GSE evals.

## 9 — Agents and autonomous workflows

### 48. ReAct: Synergizing Reasoning and Acting in Language Models
- **Citation:** Shunyu Yao, Jeffrey Zhao, Dian Yu, Nan Du, Izhak Shafran, Karthik Narasimhan, Yuan Cao — Princeton University and Google. ICLR (2023), 2022. arXiv:2210.03629 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.03629 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2210.03629v3
- **File:** [Yao_2022_react_synergizing_reasoning_acting_language.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Yao_2022_react_synergizing_reasoning_acting_language.pdf) — **33 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** ReAct (Yao et al. — Princeton/Google): interleaving reasoning traces with actions lets agents plan, use tools, and recover from errors better than action-only or reasoning-only baselines (ICLR 2023).
- **Why it matters:** The primary source for the fleet's operating pattern — reason, act, observe, repeat — with the receipts to justify it.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** Fleet/automation (agent loop standard); GSE research lanes
- **Second-order uses:** ReAct + Reflexion (2303.11366) is the fleet's full loop: interleave in the moment, reflect across episodes.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Agent design everywhere.
- **Verdict:** ADOPT — **First use:** Name ReAct as the loop pattern in the fleet's agent-brief template, with its HotpotQA/FEVER evidence cited.

### 49. Reflexion: Language Agents with Verbal Reinforcement Learning
- **Citation:** Noah Shinn, Federico Cassano, Edward Berman, Ashwin Gopinath, Karthik Narasimhan, Shunyu Yao — Northeastern University and MIT. NeurIPS (2023), 2023. arXiv:2303.11366 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.11366 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2303.11366v4
- **File:** [Shinn_2023_reflexion_language_agents_verbal_reinforcement.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Shinn_2023_reflexion_language_agents_verbal_reinforcement.pdf) — **19 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** Reflexion (Shinn et al. — Northeastern/MIT): agents that verbally reflect on failures and store the reflections in episodic memory improve across trials without weight updates (NeurIPS 2023).
- **Why it matters:** It is the published version of Garrett's gap-audit doctrine: failures become written memory that changes the next attempt. Direct support for the fleet's self-correction loops.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** Fleet/automation (post-run reflection artifacts); QA/QC loops
- **Second-order uses:** Reflexion memory + the completion-gap-audit skill: reflections are only useful if indexed — pair the mechanism with the house filing discipline.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Any agent operation; also a human-team practice (blameless postmortems).
- **Verdict:** ADOPT — **First use:** Require a Reflexion-style failure note (what failed, causal guess, next-trial change) at the end of every major lane report.

### 50. SWE-agent: Agent-Computer Interfaces Enable Automated Software Engineering
- **Citation:** John Yang, Carlos E. Jimenez, Alexander Wettig, Kilian Lieret, Shunyu Yao, Karthik Narasimhan, Ofir Press — Princeton University. NeurIPS (2024), 2024. arXiv:2405.15793 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15793 · PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2405.15793v3
- **File:** [Yang_2024_swe_agent_agent_computer_interfaces.pdf](sandbox://workspace/research-pdfs-2026-10-30/pdfs/Yang_2024_swe_agent_agent_computer_interfaces.pdf) — **118 pages**, full text extracted and read (abstract, methods, key results).
- **What it proves/builds:** SWE-agent (Princeton): the agent-computer interface itself is a performance variable — purpose-built tools and feedback formats substantially raise issue-resolution rates on SWE-bench.
- **Why it matters:** Interface design for agents is a lever Garrett controls directly (his prompts, tools, and file formats); this paper prices that lever.
- **Maps to Garrett's systems:** Fleet/automation (tool/interface design); Jules lane operations
- **Second-order uses:** SWE-agent + SWE-bench (2310.06770): the agent and its yardstick — local runs of both give the fleet a measured coding capability curve.
- **Transfer beyond sports:** Agent tooling design across the industry.
- **Verdict:** ADAPT — **First use:** Audit one fleet tool interface against SWE-agent's design lessons (concise feedback, guardrails, search-first) and log the deltas.

## Gap audit
- **Located, not downloaded (not counted):** Carpenter et al., 'Stan: A Probabilistic Programming Language' (Journal of Statistical Software, 2017) — journal PDF located; the 50 slots filled first with arXiv-verified items of equal weight (NUTS, VI review, PyMC/NumPyro/Pyro cover the same machinery). Rasmussen & Williams, 'Gaussian Processes for Machine Learning' — book, not legally open as a full PDF. Herbrich et al., TrueSkill (NeurIPS 2006, Microsoft) — publisher PDF located; rating slots were filled with deeper Bradley-Terry/state-space sources.
- **Provenance cross-check caveat:** OpenAlex was queried for institution/venue verification but its API was unresponsive in this environment (requests hung); provenance therefore rests on the affiliations printed on each PDF's first pages plus venue/journal records — both captured per item above. A later OpenAlex pass can add citation counts without changing inclusion.
- **Kaggle:** no institutional dataset on Kaggle cleared the bar ahead of the arXiv corpus picks within this hunt's timebox; Kaggle is logged as a searched-but-unfilled source, not silently skipped.
- **Navier-Stokes catalog overlap caveat:** the Oct 17, 2026 400-paper NS/fluid catalog (commit 8d88883a4, branch research/layer2-weeks1-5-reasoning-ingestion-2026-10-16) is not present as a title list in the reachable workspace files, so §1's six physics papers could not be title-checked against all 400. They are canonical methods papers (PINNs, FNO, DeepONet, SINDy, GNS, Neural ODE) read here at full-text level — if any also sits in that catalog, this hunt's contribution is the deep read, per the follow-up brief's rule.
- **Paywalls:** no counted item is paywalled; every counted PDF is the legal open version from arXiv.
- **Reading depth:** all 50 were text-extracted in full (extraction sizes in metadata.json); dossiers rest on abstract + methods + results review. They are working dossiers, not substitutes for the program's full ledger-grade deep reads.
